# Changelog

All notable changes to the MLM VPN Android app are documented here. Dates are in `YYYY-MM-DD`. This file follows the spirit of [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/); version numbers match `versionName` in [`app/build.gradle`](app/build.gradle).

فارسی این فایل در ادامه (پایین همین صفحه) آمده است.

## [1.2.45] — 2026-10-30

### Added
- **Gereh: search in Gereh and in Telegram.** Searching the chat list now also finds Gereh's channels and groups, under «Search in Gereh»: those closest to your words first, then the bigger and older ones. A tap joins (a channel at once, a group once its admins approve). A channel's or group's owner can turn «Show in search» off on its edit page. Under «Search in Telegram», Telegram's public channels come through your own Telegram script, and one tap adds them to Gereh. If you made the script before this version, put its new code into Google once.
- **Gereh: Telegram channels in Gereh.** Add a public Telegram channel by its name from the «New message» menu. Gereh reads its posts, photos and videos through your own Google script and keeps them only on your phone; the phone never connects to Telegram. «Update» brings new posts, on a timer if you like. You choose whether files come too, up to what size, and now or later, and Gereh tells you the data and requests it used.
- **Gereh: the app's tiles and settings in chats.** From the attach menu («MLMVPN» and «Settings») send one of the app's tiles or settings; it shows as a card with an «Open in MLMVPN» button, and one tap opens that very part of the app for whoever gets it. Each phone draws the card itself, so it never needs downloading, even when forwarded.
- **Gereh: a «Configs» tab on a group's or channel's info page.** Every config in a chat (links, NPV, OpenVPN, WireGuard, Amnezia and lists) in one tab beside «Media» and «Links». The media, files, voice, music, links and GIF tabs, which were empty, now fill as well.
- **Gereh: «Mute» on a member's message, and timed mutes.** In a group, tapping a member's message offers «Mute»: from 30 minutes to a week, or for good, and «Unmute» for someone muted. Every admin of the official group can too. A muted member sees how long is left.
- **Gereh: slow mode in the official group.** The official group's admins can let each member send only one message every so many seconds.
- **Gereh: the Gereh tile's menu.** On the Gereh tile choose whether Gereh stays in the background or works only while open, and mute all of Gereh.
- **Gereh on 32-bit phones.** Gereh now opens on older phones with a 32-bit processor (armeabi-v7a) as well; before, it said it needed a 64-bit phone. On such a phone install this release's «armeabi-v7a» file, or the «universal» one.
- **Gereh: polls in groups and channels.** Telegram's own polls: a question with up to 10 options, one answer or several, or a quiz. Anonymous as in Telegram unless its maker turns that off. A vote can be taken back or changed, and a channel's readers vote too.
- **Gereh: files of Telegram channels.** A Telegram channel post with a file (npvs, ovpn, conf and others) gets a «Download» button; one tap brings the file and the post becomes the file itself. Up to 20 files a day per phone.
- **Gereh: «Test» and «Move» under configs in Telegram channels.** A config in a Telegram channel post is tested on the spot or moved to the right part of the app, like the configs in chats.
- **Gereh: NPV files in chats.** An npvs or npvt file gets «Open in NPV Tunnel» and «Move», and on the NPV page the files you got come first, under their own names.
- **Gereh: choose a relay.** The two arrows at the top of Gereh list the relays and how each stands: «Automatic», «Measure all relays», or one of your choice. The one you choose is always open and goes first; the others stay as its backup, so a chat never stops.
- **Gereh: slow mode in ordinary groups.** An admin allowed to ban members can let each member send only one message every so many seconds, from the group's «Permissions»; a member sees how long is left.
- **Gereh: the list of removed users in a group.** In a group's «Permissions», «Removed users» lists everyone banned; a tap on one lifts the ban.
- **Gereh: an «old version» tag.** Beside someone who has not updated the app, in messages, under their name and in member lists, «old version» shows, and you are told when yours is old.
- **Gereh: clear its data, delete the account, or remove Gereh.** From the Gereh tile's menu: clear Gereh's messages and files, delete the account, or take Gereh out of the app entirely; «Bring Gereh back» is in the app's settings.
- **Gereh: one official channel and group with Dr.Net.** MLMVPN's and Dr.Net's users now share one official channel and one official group, each app showing it under its own name.
- **Support Gereh.** The heart beside Gereh's menu: if Gereh has been useful to you, a small contribution helps its development, infrastructure and new features.

### Fixed
- **Gereh: joining by a link said «request sent» while it had not arrived.** A request to join a group or channel whose admins approve members waits until the phone that made the link is reachable. Gereh now says it is in the queue instead of «sent», and it reaches the admins within seconds once that phone is online.
- **Gereh: a channel's subscribers are for its admins only.** As in Telegram, only a channel's admins see who subscribed; everyone else sees the number.
- **Gereh: the official chats froze the screens and chats did not load.** Every name on the screen made Gereh rebuild the official channel or group from all its posts and reactions and check each signature again, holding Gereh up for seconds at a time. Now it is built once and kept until something changes: on a Galaxy A50 Gereh is held up about 2% of the time instead of nearly half (in 32-bit mode 5% instead of four fifths).
- **Gereh froze and closed on many phones.** Gereh's screens waited for the official chats' sync, and Gereh's background service then could not start in time, so Android closed it. The sync is now light and off the main thread: every 45 seconds while an official chat is open and every 10 minutes while the app is in front; posts still come from contacts at once.
- **Gereh used the battery all day.** Off screen, Gereh no longer scans for Bluetooth all the time (other phones still find yours) or keeps Wi-Fi awake for every broadcast on the network, and the official group is muted until you turn it on (the official channel still notifies). With the flood above gone, Gereh stays almost idle in the background. The Bluetooth connection is now off by default, with its switch in your profile.
- **Gereh: others' messages came only after you sent one.** A connection the network had silently dropped looked alive, so nothing arrived until your own send noticed. Now it is noticed within about a minute and opened again, and what waited arrives at once.
- **Gereh: no web links or @channels from members in the official group.** The group had filled with ads: a member's post with a web link, t.me link, domain or @channel is now refused and shown nowhere. Config links (vless, ss, trojan and the rest) stay allowed, and admins may post anything.
- **Gereh: the official channel and group showed no photo.** Their photo was kept on the relay for two weeks at most; now it is kept for good and loads for everyone.
- **Gereh: messages and downloads through the Iran relay stopped.** Each official post was copied to every contact of the last day and sent over the relay, which filled it. Now a post goes only to contacts reached in the last 10 minutes; the rest read it from the official copy. The relay itself now has a storage cap and cleans up the oldest data by itself.
- **Gereh: a file is at most 20 MB.** In chats, groups, channels and the official chats; Gereh says so before sending.
- **Gereh: «Pin» in the message menu.** The owner and the admins allowed to pin, in groups, channels and the official chats, and both sides of a one-to-one chat, now find «Pin» when they tap a message; the option had stayed hidden.
- **Gereh: «Saved Messages».** What you send to yourself is now kept on this phone and no longer gets a «!», and this chat's texts were corrected.
- **Gereh: search tabs that did nothing are gone.** «Apps» and «Posts» left search: Gereh has no mini apps, and no phone can search the posts of chats it is not in.
- **Gereh: chats stopped whenever the Iran relay had trouble.** One «busy» answer from the Iran relay kept a phone off it until the app restarted, everyone's card told contacts of the Iran relay alone, and a restart of the relay that took seconds cut chats for ten minutes. Now the Iran relay is tried again within seconds, messages also go through the other relays a phone reads and the Google relays, and files do not cost several times the data.
- **Gereh: official posts at once.** Each official post goes onto the Iran relay once, and every phone gets it the moment it is there, even one with no contacts.
- **Gereh: forwarding works in every direction.** Forwarding from channel to channel, from a Telegram channel to a chat or group, from a group to a chat or channel and between chats always ended in «!»; now the message goes again from this phone, without «forwarded from».
- **Gereh: a Telegram proxy link opens in Telegram.** Tapping a Telegram proxy link (MTProto or SOCKS) in Gereh opens it in the Telegram app, with an «Open in Telegram» button under it; before, Gereh turned the proxy on for itself.
- **Gereh: photos sooner, and always there.** Until a photo arrives in full, a blurred preview of it shows (groups, channels, users and the info page), and ordinary groups' and channels' photos now stay on the Iran relay and load quickly for everyone.
- **Gereh: a late post in its place in time.** An official post that arrived late sat under newer messages; now it takes its own place in time.
- **Gereh: one account per phone in the official group.** An advertiser who was removed came back with a new account from the same phone; now the ban holds for the phone. Posts from older versions are not shown in the official group: update to write there.
- **Gereh: the reported crashes fixed.** The crashes of 1.2.43 and 1.2.44 are fixed: most of all Gereh's service (about 650 reports), then voice messages in the player, the downloads list, and the app starting before the phone is first unlocked after a reboot. «Location» left the attach menu until it is built.
- **FENIX did not connect on Iranian lines.** Mozilla first asks for a browser check on Iranian lines; the check now opens for the very server that asked for it, with each site's own cookies.
- **Amnezia «connected» but carried no data.** Testing and the connection check are now a real HTTPS request through the tunnel itself, so a server that only answers pings no longer counts as connected.

## [1.2.44] — 2026-10-29

### Added
- **Gereh: pinned messages.** Pin a message in a one-to-one chat, a group, a channel, or the official channel and group: the owner and admins pin (members too where the group allows it), every member sees the pin at the top of the chat, and someone who joins later sees it as well. In the official chats only their admins pin.
- **Gereh: configs in chats.** A message with config links (VLESS, VMess, Trojan, Shadowsocks and the rest) or a config file (.conf, .npvs, .ovpn, a list in .txt) gets two buttons, under your own messages too. «Test» tries each one on your network without leaving the chat; «Move» puts them (all, or only the ones that connected) into the app's configs, in a «Gereh» folder, and «Go» opens that page.
- **Gereh: a private chat from the official group.** A post in the official group now carries its writer's card, so a tap on a member's name there opens a private chat with them.
- **Gereh: when each subscriber joined.** A channel's subscriber list shows when each one joined, instead of «just now» for everyone (Dr.Net team).

### Fixed
- **Gereh: the official group's messages came late.** They were read every 20 minutes; now every 10 seconds while the official chat is open, and every 2 minutes while the app is in front.
- **Gereh: photos, voice messages and files in the official channel and group did not download.** They kept loading forever. Now they go through the Iran relay, sealed, and stay there for 14 days.
- **Gereh: a reply in the official group reached others without its quote.** Now the quoted message shows as soon as it arrives.
- **Gereh: muting a channel or group did not stay.** It now stays muted.
- **Gereh: «Article» asked for Premium.** Formatting an article now works for everyone.
- **Gereh: messages stuck at one tick.** A phone no longer waits for a relay that never answers, and a message to someone who reinstalled Gereh no longer goes on a session their phone lost (Dr.Net team).
- **Gereh: an official chat of an older version stayed in the list.** It now goes from the list loaded at start-up too (Dr.Net team).

## [1.2.43] — 2026-10-24

### Added
- **Gereh, a phone-to-phone messenger (new tile «گره»).** A messenger with Telegram's look and features and no server at all: a message goes straight from your phone to your contact's, and everything is end-to-end encrypted. No phone number or email: your identity is a key made on the phone, with a name like «ehsan·4821». Add a contact by QR or card link, or «add nearby» over Bluetooth or Wi-Fi with a 4-emoji check. Messages arrive over a shared Wi-Fi even without internet, over Bluetooth when you are close, and over the internet through volunteers -- the users' own phones and computers. One-to-one chats, groups with an owner and admins, channels (only admins post), photos, video, files and voice messages, profile, group and channel photos, and chat folders. Notifications while the app is closed, and MLMVPN's own language with full Persian. Gereh runs in a process of its own, so its errors never touch the VPN.
- **A home-screen widget for Gereh.** Gereh's tile with the number of unread messages (a red badge) and who sent the newest one, and whether Gereh reaches anyone right now (connected, over Wi-Fi, over Bluetooth, waiting for network); a tap opens Gereh. Add it from Widget Studio › «ویجت روش‌ها» or from the phone's own widget list. No message text ever reaches the home screen, and with a Gereh passcode the sender's name is hidden too.
- **Gereh: invite links and the official channel and group.** A group or channel now has an invite link («mlmchat://g/…»), with or without an admin's approval; a tap on it in any chat opens the join page. Every Gereh user is in the official channel and group «MLMVPN Official» from the first start (in the channel only its admins post, in the group anyone can write); posts pass from phone to phone. Nobody leaves or deletes these two; they can be muted or archived.
- **Gereh: the Google relay, when two phones cannot reach each other.** When no route joins two phones (one on Irancell and one on MCI, say), messages pass through a «mailbox» in the user's own Google account (an Apps Script): it works on Irancell too, the phone finds its own way to Google, and Google and the mailbox's owner see only sealed bytes. Several relays side by side, a one-tap test, each relay's numbers, and for its owner a pause and a daily device limit (Data and storage › Google relays). Photos and files go this way too, cut into alike 16 KB parts.
- **Gereh: public relays.** A relay's owner can, by their own choice, open it to everyone, so users with no relay of their own get their messages too. A phone with no relay borrows one or two public relays by itself, only after the relay itself says it is public; nobody but its owner can make it public. News of public relays spreads phone to phone with the official channel. For a relay made before this version, put the new script code in place of the old once.
- **Gereh: the member count and photo of the official channel and group.** Their member count (everyone who used Gereh lately) is estimated phone to phone, within about 2% and without revealing anyone, and spreads over the Google relay too. Nobody's joining or leaving is shown in the official group; only the number changes. Admins can now set the photo of both, and it reaches everyone.
- **Gereh: reactions, and the official channel and group fixed.** Reactions now work in one-to-one chats, groups, channels and the official channel and group (hold a message, or tap it twice): one per person per message, channel readers too. In the official chats: admins change the name and description and it reaches everyone; a deleted post goes from the other phones too (posts deleted before this version as well); photos and files can be posted there; a new user gets the posts and the member count from their first contact within seconds, not ten minutes later; and the member count updates at once. Google relay: pausing a relay no longer blocks its owner's own messages, opening a paused relay to everyone no longer says the script is old, and the script code survives copying (for a relay made before, put the new code in place of the old once).
- **Gereh: group and channel settings.** A group's or channel's description, members' default rights (who may send text, photos, files, links and the rest, who may add members or pin) and deleting a group or channel for everyone work. The rows that need Telegram's server (public link, boosts and colours, stickers, statistics, translation and the like) are hidden.
- **Russian and Chinese (Русский, 简体中文).** Two new languages for the whole app and for Gereh, chosen in Settings › App Language; Gereh follows it with «Same as MLMVPN» or takes its own. In this version the menus, buttons and messages are translated (about 5,600 of the app's texts and all of Gereh's own); part of the text inside some pages, and Telegram's original screens in Gereh, are still in English and follow in the next versions.
- **Outline: Shadowmere's keys, tagged and timed.** Outline now also takes every key shadowmere.xyz lists. Shadowmere checks all its keys itself and hands out only the working ones. As the site is filtered in Iran, the app ships with its 186 keys of 2026-10-25 and updates them only through one of the app's tunnels: by themselves whenever Outline connects, and on «Refresh the lists» while any tile's tunnel is on (Outline, V2Ray, WireGuard or any other); with no tunnel on, the app says to turn one on first. Each of these keys carries the «shadowmere» tag in its name and on its row, with the time Shadowmere last updated it and how often it passed. A «Shadowmere» row on the Outline page and a «shadowmere» chip in the server list show them on their own; «Test all shadowmere» (and «Quick test») pings every one of them, and «Connect to the fastest» then connects to the fastest that answered. Nothing is pinged by itself, only when you tap a test.

### Changed
- **Newer build tools.** The app is built with AGP 8.13, Kotlin 2.1 and compileSdk 36 (for Gereh's code). targetSdk stays 34, so the VPN behaves as before. The app is larger because of Gereh.
- **Aether core 2.4.0.** The Aether core behind «وارپ», the Aether screen, the exit-country ping and Game Boost is now its developer's 2.4.0: MASQUE connects sooner where the WARP API is filtered (WARP is no longer switched on again at every connect), making a WARP identity also tries Cloudflare edges when the API's own addresses are blocked, the MASQUE scan tries only known working gateways, on every port, and a tunnel that ran for hours reconnects at once to the same server.
- **Aether: two new ways to connect and to make a WARP identity.** From PattNG: when MASQUE connects over neither HTTP/3 nor HTTP/2, the Aether screen tries the semi-python ClientHello with fragmenting, then the same ClientHello through Xray's tlshello, and keeps the one that connected for that network. Making a WARP identity (for «ماسک», «وایرگارد», «وارپ در وارپ» and «وارپ») tries the same two ways when ECH and the direct way fail, and remembers per network the way that worked.

### Fixed
- **The app was closed in the background for its CPU use.** Android closed the app in the background when a process (an engine core such as Lantern, Tor or Geph, or a test process) still worked after you left its page with no VPN up (about 80 reports). Now, two minutes after the app goes to the background with no VPN and no foreground service running, those processes end and a scan stops. Nothing is touched while a VPN is connected.
- **Freezes on the home screen and a few other pages.** Slow work moved off the main thread: the home tiles' artwork (now decoded at display size), the connection widget's last method, the disconnect command, the traffic file at connect, the app icons of Anti-sanction, NPV's best-config pick, and stopping Game Booster. On slow phones each of these froze the app for nine seconds or more.
- **FENIX did not connect on older phones.** Firefox VPN's servers use a certificate from a root (GlobalSign E46/R46) that older Androids (Android 11, for one) do not have, so every connection to them failed on those phones while the same account worked on a newer one. FENIX now carries those two roots itself, next to the phone's own.
- **Fewer freezes on slow phones.** The connect button of the home widget, disconnecting a tunnel, the home wallpaper, the Scout page and MAE's app icons no longer hold the main thread, and a connect that reached the tunnel service without its configuration no longer closes the app.
- **A few more crashes.** Devices without the VPN permission dialog (some TV boxes and projectors) now get a message; stopping in the middle of a Lantern or Psiphon check no longer closes the app; Proton on a phone without WebView; URnetwork on Android 7; copying a very large list in the scanner; an incompatible GST core; an Xray core's process dying; and a config name with a «%».
- **A few Gereh faults.** The «this part of Gereh is not built yet» flash on opening the official channel and group and their info pages; profile, group and channel photos showing no thumbnail; a new group or channel missing from the list after going back; the line under a contact's name saying «on this Wi-Fi» after a message through the relay; the «colour themes» strip spinning for ever (now the themes and colours built into the app, offline); the day/night button sticking after one use; the empty three-dot button in Settings; the «app icon» section and privacy options with nothing behind them are hidden; and a clear message when a relay's script code on Google has an error.

## [1.2.42] — 2026-10-17

### Important
- **The Cloudflare problem is fixed -- one clean-up is needed.** First delete EVERYTHING the app made in your Cloudflare account: every Worker, every KV namespace and every D1 database (Cloudflare dashboard). Then deploy again from the app, and in the Cloud section press «سنجش این خط» (the «سنجش روش‌ها روی این شبکه» button on the «روش اتصال این شبکه» card) so the app measures the way to Cloudflare on your line.

### Added
- **Lantern, a new engine (tile «لنترن»).** The official Lantern 10 core (radiance, built for Android arm64 without its VPN layer: `jniLibs/arm64-v8a/liblantern.so`, ~21 MB in the APK) behind the app's own tunnel, as on Windows: all traffic or smart mode (`-smart`; Iranian sites go direct, resolved with the phone's own DNS via `-localdns`), «فهرست سرور تازه» when the kept list stops answering, the data path proven and the exit country shown once connected. When the line cannot reach Lantern's config service the first time, the first list is fetched through the WARP leg (`-fetchvia`) and the traffic itself still goes straight to Lantern's servers. Warm connects take 5-6 s. Lantern is measured in MLM Scout. 32-bit phones show «not available» instead of crashing. The tile sits after Geph and is moved onto existing boards once.
- **URnetwork, a new engine (tile «URnetwork»).** URnetwork's own official SDK: traffic leaves through several «provider» devices in different countries at once. The first time it shows only URnetwork's terms and a «Create account» button (no email; a recovery phrase). While connecting, every step and the providers (being checked, connected out of 12) are shown live; the data card shows used, left and the next grant of the free allowance, which is daily (00:00 UTC). Ad and tracker blocking (off by default), the exit country changed without dropping the tunnel, Smart Iran and MTU from the app's settings, and the official app's power saving while battery saver is on. Moving between Wi-Fi and mobile data keeps the providers. In MAE and MLM Scout too; 64-bit phones only.
- **NPV, a new section (tile «NPV»).** Configs from NPV channels and your own, in one place. An npvt, npvs or text file comes in from the page, the phone's «Open with» or share (`ui/npv/NpvImportActivity`), or a link is pasted from the clipboard. «بررسی کانفیگ‌های تازه» reads the 100 default Telegram channels, the user's own channels and public lists -- reaching Telegram directly, through another VPN or through this app's own tunnel -- puts what is new in the «انبار» store and, after asking, tests everything and removes the broken ones. The healthy ones are marked and sent to the main page, where «اتصال به سریع‌ترین» connects on the app's V2Ray engine (`MyVpnService`, like Outline's keys) and another tap tries the next config, so split tunnelling, LAN sharing and the Smart Iran route apply. NPV Tunnel's own npvt/npvs files are encrypted with that app's key: they are kept and handed to NPV Tunnel, never decrypted here. Fetching files from Telegram by itself needs a Telegram sign-in that is not built yet; for now a tap opens the post in Telegram. In MLM Scout too (`NpvAdapter`). Before 2026-10-20 a release APK left out both this tile and URnetwork's (`HomeDestinations.IN_PROGRESS`), widgets included; both now ship.
- **Search on the home screen, the iPhone way.** The page dots became a small glass pill (with «جستجو» on a board of one page); a tap brings Spotlight down over the wallpaper with the keyboard up. Tiles come first -- on the board, in folders and in the dock -- by Persian or English name or another word for them, then, under a line, the matching rows of Settings, which open on their page with the row lit up. Persian matching treats ي/ی, ك/ک, the half-space, tatweel, diacritics and Persian/Arabic/Latin digits as the same and ranks a prefix above a substring (`ui/home/HomeSearch.kt`, `SearchMatcher.kt`, `ui/settings/SettingsIndex.kt`).
- **Home-screen widgets for every connection method.** Each of the 27 connection tiles («ماسک», «وایرگارد» … «ضد فیلتر SNI») has a widget of its own for the phone's home screen, drawn from the tile's own artwork: one tap connects or disconnects that method (the VPN permission, a running scan and a phone without internet are handled; the connect is carried by a short foreground service so Android cannot freeze it half-way, and a failure says so in a notification that opens the method's page), a lamp shows connected/connecting, and the size the launcher gives picks the layout (an icon, the icon with name and state, or a large card). Tool tiles (help, settings, store…) have none. Added from the launcher's widget list or from Widget Studio (`widget/`). On the phone (One UI): Psiphon connected from a widget in ~5 s with the chosen exit (DE), WireGuard in ~3 s, a tap on another method's widget takes the first down and brings the second up.
- **Widget Studio (new tile «ویجت استدیو»).** Widgets of your own design: 8 looks (glass, minimal, neon, colourful, compact, dark pro, iOS, wave), three sizes, accent and background colours, glassiness, corner radius, text size, the button (ring, pill, solid, glow), the method and its exit country, and what it shows (status, country, live speed, usage, time, chart). Designs are saved, any number of widgets can wear them, and saving redraws every placed one; the preview is drawn by the same painter the home screen gets. The studio also holds the plain method widgets' settings (card, name, lamp, tap connects) and the switch of the in-app widget. Designs travel in Backup (`widget/studio/`, `ui/widgetstudio/`).
- **A connection widget at the top of the app's home board.** iOS-style, two rows high on the first page: the method that is up, its exit country, a live traffic chart, this session's usage and a disconnect button; idle, it offers the last method in one tap. The «−» in edit mode removes it; Settings › Display («ویجت») and Widget Studio turn it back on (`ui/home/HomeConnectionWidget.kt`).
- **Backup and restore (Settings › «پشتیبان‌گیری و بازگردانی»).** Three kinds of export: the main settings; accounts and configs (Cloudflare, WARP identities, Geph, FENIX, Proton, Windscribe, GitHub, OpenVPN, configs and subscriptions…); and each method's own settings, method by method. One `.mlmbak` file, optionally sealed with a password (AES-256-GCM, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 310k), saved with the system file picker or sent with the share sheet -- nothing is uploaded. Secrets the phone's Keystore seals travel opened inside the file and are sealed again with the new phone's keys on restore (without a password the export warns that whoever holds the file holds the accounts). The restore shows what the file holds, merges or replaces, stops the VPN, and is applied at the next start before anything reads the settings, all or nothing (a journal rolls a half-done restore back). Every preferences file in the code has a written backup decision (`BackupCoverageTest`).
- **MAE: the exit abroad is the account's central Worker.** The Worker exit is the central Worker every panel rides (BPB's id), and MAE deploys no Worker of its own any more; «راه‌اندازی ورکر مرکزی» adopts the one already on the account. Its Proxy IP lets the exit reach Cloudflare-hosted sites. MAE's old Worker can be removed under Manage; when the Worker changes, every app is checked once again.
- **MAE: fixed exits abroad for Gemini and Google Flow.** These two open only from one fixed server abroad, so they are given only a fixed exit (`Capabilities.stableExit`, `PolicyEngine.fixedExitAbroad`): never the Worker or Lantern, whose exit moves, and «not found» instead of a false «ready». New exits: FENIX and FENIX-US beside the chosen country, the Outline servers that worked on this network, and Proton and Windscribe WireGuard servers with junk packets in front (two each plus one in the US). The US helper (the Gemini exit Durable Object) is gone from MAE. The exits not set up are listed on the Manage page, on every app's route list and under an app that found no exit abroad, with an orange «not set up» line and a tap to their tile; FENIX only needs an account -- MAE takes it by itself. Proxy IP cannot carry Google (measured: 0 of 12 US proxy IPs reached it), so it is not offered for these two.
- **MAE: Lantern as an exit.** While MAE is connected, a Lantern core of its own runs for MAE (`files/mae/lantern/v10`, its list copied from the tile) and stays up through MAE's own reconnects; off on phones under 2.5 GB of memory and switchable on the Lantern page («استفاده در MAE»).
- **Psiphon level with Windows.** Six methods (domain fronting, clean IP, all protocols, relay through other users, personal Conduit, proxy server) with automatic trying them in turn and remembering the winner by name per mode; a capacity check after connecting (six streams; a server under 0.9 Mbit/s is replaced, up to three per method); a live card (method, protocol, capacity, drops, clean IP, proxy). Clean IPs: Akamai and Fastly edges harvested from Psiphon's own fronting names, a TLS scan, then a real test with a throw-away Psiphon; proxy server: a form with authentication, or a ready list by country, tested again right before connecting; personal Conduit pairing links.
- **Outline and Amnezia without caps.** The 1,500 and 3,000 server caps are gone; a dead server no source lists any more is dropped after three days, while the user's own, favourites and ones that worked stay.
- **IPv4 exit country for WARP, WireGuard and MASQUE**, as on Windows: a free HTTP/SOCKS proxy chained after WARP (`SocksFront`), a seed of 47,947 proxies in 145 countries, each tested from inside WARP, the exit confirmed with Cloudflare's trace after connecting, a dead proxy replaced (up to six times) and watched every 60 s. NAT64 and IPv4 are never on together.
- **MASQUE falls back to WireGuard.** MASQUE gets 30 s (60 s once the log shows it accepted), then WireGuard connects by itself with the same exit country and a card says which one is up; MASQUE is skipped on that network for 12 hours.

### Changed
- **Psiphon clean IPs: the real test is ten times faster and more accurate.** A one-second data pre-check first (TLS with a random SNI, then a request with a Host of Psiphon's own meek fronts: an edge that answers with the meek server's 404 carries data, one that answers nothing does not -- zero false negatives against the full test on 309 IPs), test processes by the phone's memory (1 to 6) that live for the whole round, only the same CDN's fronting servers in each test and the tunnel's real address checked (a Fastly server reached through DNS had made a dead Akamai IP «connected»). 309 IPs: about 7 minutes instead of about 70. A floating progress card (counts, tests in flight with their seconds, time left, «⚡ fastest so far» with «انتخاب», Stop), a lazy list sorted by speed while it runs, no multi-second frames, Stop frees the screen at once.
- **Outline: tests start at once and only when asked, and many more servers pass.** Opening the page only refreshes the list. A direct TCP knock (64 workers, 3 s) feeds the real stream test immediately; the servers that do not answer directly are tested again from inside WARP with the app's own WARP leg brought up from the start (it found twice as many as the internal carrier: 188 against 92 of 2,213 on our line).

### Fixed
- **«سنجش این خط» finds the way on more lines.** On a tester's line it answered «no method worked» while a way existed. The measurement now takes a few seconds' look first -- ECH, F&F and the link as written on several Cloudflare edges, and Chrome on IPv6, all at once -- and proves what passed there first, then the three ways of the 2026-10 summary (ECH, IPv6, F&F) in that line's order. Before, each stage had 90 s for one strategy at a time and a failing one costs ~30 s, so on a line of the other firewall type only two or three of ~30 were tried and IPv6 was never reached. Also: the ECH key is fetched by the app (plain DNS to four resolvers, then DNS over HTTPS) instead of only by the core over UDP to 1.1.1.1, which some lines drop; the summary's addresses (188.114.98.0, 2a06:98c1:3121::7) are always among the edges; a worker name that resolves to a block page's address (10.10.34.x) is dialled on a working Cloudflare edge. When no Cloudflare way works, WARP («وایرگارد») and MASQUE («ماسک») get their quick check (MLM Scout) and the answer names the tile to connect with.
- **An IPv6 exit country that connected and carried nothing.** A NAT64 gateway can pass WireGuard's handshake and data-plane check and then bring nothing back: on the test phone Amsterdam's gateway did exactly that (the phone kept sending, not one UDP datagram came back), and the tile said «متصل» with 0 B received for two minutes, until the core retried the same gateway. Now a pinned gateway has 15 s after connecting to show the core's exit address or 4 KB received; otherwise the connect moves to the country's next gateway, and a country with no working gateway is no longer shown as connected. «سریع‌ترین کشور» no longer picks by round trip alone: a gateway whose ping answered the trace but could not carry the download goes after every one that did (London had won on 220 ms; a 2 MB download through it took 8.2 s against 3.5 s through Frankfurt), and the country list is sorted the same way. The Smart Iran route was not the cause: the gateway stayed silent with it off too.
- **The app's own update could not finish, and every try began again from zero.** `UpdateNet.client` bounds the whole call (`callTimeout` = connect + read + 20 s), and the APK download used it: 95 s for the entire file, so the 1.2.41 arm64 APK (102.7 MB) needed 8.6 Mbit/s sustained -- users saw it run to the middle or near the end and drop. The download was one request with no retry, and the next try deleted the part. Now downloads have no whole-call ceiling (the 60 s read timeout still catches a dead connection) and the APK goes through the store's downloader: the part is kept (tagged with the release's size and digest) and continued with HTTP Range, a drop that moved the file forward does not count against the URL (three that bring nothing do), 5xx/429 are retried, a `Content-Range` that does not start where the part ends restarts the file instead of splicing it, and the file takes its name only when its size and GitHub's SHA-256 of the asset match. The background fill and the button share one download, and the button's download runs past the screen, so leaving the page no longer loses the installer. Measured on the test phone: resumed from 30 MB, and from 52.9 MB after the app was force-stopped mid-file; the finished file's SHA-256 matched GitHub's. The update page also stopped showing the notes' `##` and `**`.
- **The top crash of 1.2.41 -- the app closing on connect, disconnect and tests -- is gone at its root.** Xray (libgojni) ran inside the app's own process: the VPN's core, MAE's probe core, and the test cores of FLUX, the node list, Quick Connect, the free-config importer, Windscribe's WireGuard test, the WireGuard scanner, country and platform checks. A Go panic in any of them (Outline/Shadowsocks servers, WireGuard teardown, a closed tun descriptor) aborted the whole app -- over a thousand reports ("native crash SIGABRT in libgojni.so", and the same crash filed under libwebviewchromium/libpgjni/no library). Every core now runs in a process of its own (`XrayHosts`: `:xtun` for the VPN, `:maeprobe`, `:xtest`; delay tests in `:xmeasure`); a panic ends that process only, a connected tunnel reconnects by itself (three deaths in two minutes end the connection instead of looping), and the VPN's core gets its own copy of the tun descriptor, so the app and the core never close the same descriptor twice. The teardown order is also fixed (core first, then the interface).
- **Freezes and "app not responding" on big lists and busy screens.** The node list sorted by reading every number with a regex inside every comparison and wrote each test result into a map that compared itself whole; it now reads each node once and writes copy-on-write. Link parsing reads a link's query once instead of nineteen times. Moved off the main thread: the free-config import and its test, the scanner's combine, the Quick Connect list, Smart Iran's app icons, the SNI auto-rename, Proton's WebView proxy and sign-in check, Geph's account check (no Keystore on the main thread), Tor bridges, FLUX's country list, the arena history and the tunnel log file. Home tiles load their artwork once per process.
- **Out of memory with many configs.** The config list is stored in a file of its own, written and read a node at a time (it was one string of tens of megabytes in the preferences), with a backup one write back; the SNI config list shows 150 rows at a time; weak phones skip the decorative offscreen fades.
- **tun2socks "Assertion failed" (Psiphon, WireGuard, Tor) closed the app.** The library was built with debug assertions; it is rebuilt as a release build (as Psiphon ships it) with the over-acknowledgement it tripped on handled.
- **ForegroundServiceDidNotStartInTime** (Telegram bridge, MLM Scout, the tunnel stack): every start command is now answered with startForeground.
- **Crashes on phones and TV boxes without a browser, a file picker or WebView** (Windscribe sign-up, OpenVPN import, FENIX and Proton pages) are now a short message.
- **Smaller crashes:** OpenVPN account choice (Integer/Long sort key), the network-interface list (Android NPE), the Quick Settings tile refresh under app cloners, the scanner's group list (ConcurrentModificationException), VPN permission asked after leaving a screen, Android TV remote focus, out of threads when a test process died.
- **Killed in the background for CPU use.** No core runs in the app's process any more, Geph's helper engine stops when the app leaves the screen, and the tunnel's status broadcasts no longer go to helper processes (which piled up in frozen ones: "can't deliver broadcast").
- **Crash reports are more honest.** A slow moment is a breadcrumb; only a main thread stuck for 9 s is a freeze report (with a second sample). By-design exits of helper processes, memory reclaim in the background and app-cloner providers are not filed as crashes.

## [1.2.41] — 2026-10-05

### Added
- **The central Worker: one relay Worker per Cloudflare account for every panel, shared with the Windows app.** BPB is the Worker itself; Edge, Zeus, Spider, Netra, Gozargah, Nova, Nahan and MLM are one more id each on the same Worker (it accepts up to 16), so connecting a panel that is not installed deploys no Worker and no panel code (`engines/gateway/`, the Worker's seven modules in `assets/edge_gateway/`, the same files the Windows app deploys). The id and tunnel path are Worker secrets that cannot be read back, so the account's own panel registry (KV `mlmvpn-panels`, key `GW`, `docs/PANEL-REGISTRY.md`) is how the two apps learn them from each other: every change reads it first (the other device's Worker is used, never a second one), writes it BEFORE the upload (so an upload from either device can never revoke an id that is live on the other) and puts it back if Cloudflare refuses the upload; a registry that cannot be written never stops an install. The Cloud tab shows the central Worker as BPB's row and a «connect» row for every panel that is not installed as a full panel; those installed the old way keep their own rows. The links are built on the phone (no request to the Worker) and are byte-for-byte the ones the Windows app builds (`GatewayParityTest` pins them and the code hash against fixtures the Windows manager generates). Settings are a page of their own (ports, clean IPs/domains, resolved addresses, IPv6, ECH/F&F method links, the Proxy IP) and are shared too. A new fetch replaces that panel's group. The arena and the Doctor connect panels to the central Worker instead of deploying them, and the coach's EDG step does the same. **MLM on the phone is Config Studio's engine**, so MLM keeps its place: connecting it to the central Worker is separate from the full install, which stays one tap away.
- **«روش اتصال این شبکه»: a card above the Cloudflare usage chart.** One button measures which way of reaching Cloudflare works on this network with the engine the app already has (the SCAN and CONNECT stages, on the central Worker's own link when there is one), shows the winner in plain words with its age, and the strategy store keeps it for the network — the scanner, the connect, the health tests and the combine centre already read it from there. `cs=semi-python` and `fm=tlshello-0-len` (and `tlshello-5-94-1`) in a link now expand to the full values, so a config copied from the Windows app works here.
- **Deploying and taking configs go DIRECT; a tunnel only when Cloudflare is blocked.** For `api.cloudflare.com` and `*.workers.dev` / `*.pages.dev`, the registry, the central Worker, Spider, Netra, Gozargah, Nova, Nahan, MLM, Config Studio and the usage chart: OkHttp is given the routes «direct, then the local SOCKS5 port of whichever tunnel is on» (`CfTunnelSelector`; its route database makes the next call try the tunnel first without paying the direct timeout again), the name is sent to the tunnel unresolved, a direct connect gives up after 6 s and the direct route tries two addresses while a tunnel is on (`CfFront`, `WorkerRoute.dns`). Nothing changes when no tunnel is on. When Cloudflare cannot be reached at all, one sentence says to turn on one of the tunnels.
- **Telegram Proxy, a new home tile: a Telegram proxy on your own Cloudflare account, built in one tap and added to Telegram in a second.** Telegram's official «WEB proxy» (Telegram for Android 12.10+, Desktop 7.1+) runs on a Worker or on Pages of the user's own account: «Build with Workers» (ready in seconds) or «Build with Pages» (a minute or two). The app makes the relay, switches it on, then proves it works by asking a real Telegram data centre a question through it -- once through the app's own route to Cloudflare and once through the phone's ordinary network -- and only then calls it Ready. «Add to Telegram» opens Telegram's own add-proxy sheet. Up to three proxies per method and account, each with its state, a re-check, cancel, resume of an unfinished build, and a delete that removes it from Cloudflare (only resources whose ownership marker matches are ever changed). A custom domain of the user's own, with a five-step guide that ticks each step itself, gets the proxy through networks that block workers.dev and pages.dev. The Cloudflare key is read from the account the app already holds and goes only to Cloudflare; rate limits are obeyed to the minute and nothing loops. The connection stays up: the proxy page carries Telegram over several sockets and moves to a fresh one before the old one runs out of connections, a socket that dies takes only its own connections with it, and a ping every 20 seconds catches a silently dead one -- before this, Telegram showed «connecting to proxy» every few minutes. A proxy built earlier gets the new relay by itself when the tile is opened (same address and secret). A plain MTProto (`tg://proxy`) proxy and the sponsor channel cannot run on Cloudflare (it accepts no raw TCP); see `docs/TELEGRAM-PROXY.md`.
- **Support the app.** A «حمایت» button beside the shield at the top of the home screen, and «حمایت از ما» under the description in About, open the donation page (donito.me/ehsan2noven) in the phone's own browser.
- **Telegram Proxy: a bridge with a clean IP.** «Bridge with a clean IP» on a ready proxy runs a plain MTProxy on the phone (127.0.0.1) and carries Telegram through it to the user's own relay over the cleanest Cloudflare IP. Candidates come from the app's own IP scanner, the edges already proven on this network and last time's winners; each is tried for real through the relay, and the quickest must pass the full self-test (a question to a Telegram data centre) before it is used. Telegram's own add-proxy sheet then opens with the local address, and a notification keeps the bridge alive and turns it off. It moves to a fresh session before the relay's connection budget runs out, a session that dies takes only its own connections, and when every address starts failing (a new network) it looks again without changing the port. Works with any Telegram that supports MTProxy, also where workers.dev is blocked by name.
- **Outline, a new home tile: Outline VPN keys, tested on your line, fastest first.** Free public Shadowsocks servers from six GitHub lists rebuilt every 5 minutes to every few hours (OpenRay, F0rc3Run, barry-far, Epodonios, EbraSha, ShadowsocksAggregator; all checked rebuilt on 2026-10-10), plus the user's own keys -- `ss://` and Outline's dynamic `ssconf://`, pasted, scanned from a QR, or offered when one is on the clipboard. A TCP knock on every server, then a real request through each that answered; working servers rise to the top, fastest first, per network. One button connects to the fastest and moves on to the next if a server carries no data (up to three); the card shows delay, time, exit country and exit IP, and a speed test. Swipe a server away or remove every dead one -- a bar brings them back for six seconds, and deleted servers stay out of later refreshes for two weeks. Stars, country chips, search, sort by delay, speed or newest, QR and share for each key, and each source can be switched off. The key goes to the VPN service as a plain link, so split tunneling, Local Network sharing (password and blocked devices), proxy mode and the Smart Iran route all apply. In MLM Scout too: up to 24 servers raced, the winner marked «Scout-proved» in the section. Untested on a phone.
- **Smart Iran Routing (Settings > Network).** One switch: Iranian sites and Iranian apps go direct, never through the VPN -- on every engine of the app. Three layers: Iranian apps (banks, Snapp, Divar, Bale, Rubika, the operators' apps, the app stores, every `ir.` package; each changeable by hand, Bazaar/Myket installs offered) are left entirely outside the VPN, so an app that refuses to run under a VPN never sees one; Iran's IP ranges leave the tunnel at the system level (Android 13+ excludes all ~2,900 ranges; older versions route around the large ones); and on the Xray engines every `.ir` name and ~57,000 Iranian domains (also those hosted abroad) go out by the direct route. The lists come from Chocolate4U/Iran-v2ray-rules, rebuilt daily and refreshed once a day (GitHub, then jsDelivr, then through the tunnel); copies from 2026-10-10 ship in the app. Untested on a phone.
- **Windscribe connects in Iran, every protocol.** Windscribe's servers are blackholed on many Iranian lines (on the user's home Wi-Fi, 2026-10-10: no TCP connect on any port and no ping to any Windscribe address, while its API still answers through its own failover). Each protocol now goes through Cloudflare WARP, end to end with Windscribe: WireGuard through the Amnezia engine (straight first, then inside WARP, falling back across Windscribe's nodes only), and OpenVPN TCP, Stealth, WStunnel and OpenVPN UDP through one shared WARP session (the user's own Cloudflare configs as a second way for TCP). The OpenVPN tunnel leaves WARP's address out of its routes so the carrier never loops into itself. Measured on the phone through each tunnel: WireGuard exit Oslo, 8.7 Mbit/s; OpenVPN UDP exit Paris, 8.7 Mbit/s; all five protocols connected on Frankfurt. Servers near Iran (Europe) are tried before America. The official port list now includes WStunnel (it was never asked for), and a protocol choice made right after a server choice is no longer lost.
- **Windscribe: «test every server with every protocol».** One button connects each server with each protocol for real and measures it -- five requests for ping and stability, a 1 MB download for speed -- then shows one table: the best combination on top, each server with its protocols ranked, «from inside Cloudflare» where it went that way; a tap connects exactly that combination. WireGuard is measured without bringing the VPN up (a real session in a test core), so a server takes about a minute; one server alone can be tested too. Results are kept per network.
- **Windscribe keeps long sessions up.** Every 30 s a real request goes through the tunnel; a tunnel that stops carrying data, ends, or hangs reconnecting is repaired by itself (the method that worked first, so usually seconds), again and again with a growing pause, until the user disconnects -- and never against another engine or VPN app that took over.
- **Windscribe in MLM Scout.** WireGuard to the nearest node, straight and through Cloudflare WARP, measured with Scout's common yardstick.
- **Split tunneling and Local Network sharing hold on every engine.** With sharing on, MASQUE, WireGuard and WARP-in-WARP take the local-proxy path by themselves (before, they had no listener and the share only worked after finding Proxy Mode too); Amnezia, Proton and Windscribe's WireGuard are carried by Xray, which has the listener the AmneziaWG core lacks (same WireGuard, same junk packets); an engine's whole config (MAE, FLUX, FENIX…) gets a listener on the Local Port when it ships none -- before, the LAN screen said «ready» while the share dialled nothing; an AmneziaWG-only server and VPN Gate now say honestly that they cannot share. VPN Gate now follows the split-tunnel list (it ignored it); the kill switch follows it too, so an excluded app keeps its internet while a tunnel reconnects; «only these apps» with none installed tunnels everything on every engine (the WARP tiles, Tor and Psiphon refused to connect, the Xray engines put the app inside its own tunnel).
- **MLM Scout tests every method for real, the way each one connects.** Psiphon (plain and inside WARP), Amnezia (each server kind and the user's own files, direct then inside Cloudflare), Proton (its own race of ways), MAE (the config it compiles for this network), Google Script (a real request through the relay), anti-sanction (every address of the worker, which must exit outside Iran), SNI (the front's current method, ECH, then every other method -- the user's choice is put back) and domain fronting (YouTube through the front, with the app's own certificate) were listed as «soon»; now each has a tester. A test now refuses to say «does not work» for something one setting away from working: MASQUE is tested three ways (HTTP/3, split HTTP/2, plain HTTP/2) plus the user's hand-typed endpoint, every obfuscation profile is retried, «my configs» goes on to 40 configs and a cloud panel to 12 when the first ones fail, YouTube gets a second chance before a method is called weak, and a WARP tile that is closed on the line says when a tap on it still connects through its sibling (WarpLadder). The red «قطع» tag on the MASQUE tile is gone.
- **GitHub Tunnel without Cloudflare: a GitHub account is all it takes.** The cloud server now opens its own ways in -- free public relays it dials out to (localhost.run, srv.us, serveo, localtunnel), no account anywhere -- and the phone connects to them directly, measured through the real connection. The Cloudflare Worker is now optional (one more way in, further down the page); setup is two steps: GitHub, then connect. A relay that restarts under a new name is followed. Measured from the phone's raw Iranian line (2026-10-07): localhost.run and srv.us passed the WebSocket handshake, serveo's names are DNS-blocked, localtunnel was unreliable even from abroad; a real session built and connected with no Worker.
- **MASQUE, WireGuard and WARP-in-WARP make their WARP identity where the WARP API is filtered.** Iran's filter now drops Cloudflare's WARP registration host by its name -- directly and cut into pieces alike -- so on such a line the three tiles stopped at once with "making a WARP identity is filtered". The identity is now registered by the bundled Aether engine with the name encrypted (ECH, the key looked up from cloudflare-ech.com): 2-5 s on the phone (Irancell home line, 2026-10-07), and the tunnel carried data right after. «وارپ» gets its identity the same way. The identity card says «با نام رمزگذاری‌شده (ECH)». A line where the API answers directly is unchanged.
- **The three WARP tiles stand in for each other on each internet.** A tile that does not come up within its time (MASQUE 110 s, WireGuard 70 s, WARP-in-WARP 80 s) hands the connect to the member that gets through -- MASQUE to WireGuard, WireGuard to MASQUE, WARP-in-WARP to WireGuard and then MASQUE -- and says so on screen. The internet remembers what it closed (separately with and without IPv6), so the next tap there goes straight to what works, and the closed one is tried again after 12 hours. When every member was found closed minutes ago, the tap is answered at once («اتصال سریع» را امتحان کنید) instead of scanning for three more minutes. Only on a connect the user asked for, only when the line itself is up, and never with an exit country or a hand-entered endpoint. On the phone: MASQUE (closed on that line in every form) moved to WireGuard and connected; the second tap connected in 3 s.
- **Amnezia through Cloudflare: free WireGuard servers the line blocks now connect from inside Cloudflare WARP.** On many Iranian lines UDP to a foreign server is dropped while UDP to Cloudflare's WARP edge gets through. A WireGuard server that does not answer over the line is now tried once more with Cloudflare WARP carrying its packets -- WireGuard stays end to end with the server, so the exit is the server's own country, not Cloudflare's. Measured on the phone (Irancell home line, 2026-10-07): of the same public servers 8 of 40 answered directly and 21 of 40 through Cloudflare, and a Swiss server carried 11 Mbit/s with a Swiss address. The way into Cloudflare (WARP's IPv6 or IPv4 edges, whichever this line lets through) is found per network and remembered; the app registers a WARP identity of its own from inside Cloudflare, so no shared public account is needed. Servers reached this way carry an «از راه کلادفلر» tag in the list and on the connect card, and a server that last worked this way is connected this way straight away.
- **Proton VPN picks the best way to each server, on every internet, by itself.** There is no fixed order any more: every way that might reach a server -- IPv6, the server's IPv4, plain WireGuard, and through the user's own Cloudflare configs -- is tried at once, and the first that really carries data takes the tunnel (an answer alone is not enough). Then, once every few days per internet, each other way gets one short timed download (2 MB, at most 10 s) and the fastest keeps the tunnel; the result is remembered for that internet, so the next connect starts on it. The switch happens under the running tunnel -- the connection is never dropped for it. On the phone (2026-10-07): a fresh internet connected in about 10 s, and the speed tests ran at 16-24 Mbps on a 20 Mbps line.
- **Proton VPN for internets without IPv6.** Where a line has no IPv6 and Proton's IPv4 addresses do not work, Proton now connects through the app's Cloudflare configs, with the method the Cloudflare Doctor found on that internet. In the lab 8 of 8 servers connected this way; on a simulated line without IPv6 the speed tests ran at 15-24 Mbps.
- **The Cloudflare engine remembers which kind of method works on each internet.** The methods that work on one Iranian internet often fail on another; the engine now recognises which kind the current internet needs (from what worked there before, or a quick check kept for three days) and tries those first.
- **Proton VPN, a new home tile: the free WireGuard servers of the user's own Proton account.** One button, «Sign in / create a Proton account»: Proton's official page opens inside the app, the user signs in or makes a free account there, and the app takes the account's free WireGuard servers by itself and connects with them. The password, the emailed code, the CAPTCHA and 2FA are entered only on Proton's page; the app never sees them and never tries to get past a check. Navigation inside the page is limited to Proton's own sites. Connecting uses the Amnezia engine (junk packets hide the handshake, a real request proves the connection, the exit country is learned) but only among Proton's servers: a failing Proton server never moves the user to someone else's. Proton is filtered on many Iranian lines, so before the page opens the app checks whether Proton answers: on the line as it is, or through the engine that is on (the page is pointed at that engine's local listener, because the app itself stays outside the tunnels). If it does not answer, the user turns on FLUX, Tor or WireGuard in one tap (or any other engine from its own screen) and the page opens by itself. Take as many servers as you like, press Done, and the app turns the engine off, pings the servers and connects to the fastest. A «ping all» row, the delay on every server, and a browser-plus-file fallback are on the main page. Add more servers, delete one, sign out of Proton from the screen. A free Proton account connects one device at a time. Untested on a phone: whether each network lets Proton's page and its UDP handshake through.

### Changed
- **The Cloudflare key field in the add-account form shows dots and uses the password keyboard.** Pasting still works; the keyboard no longer learns the key.
- **Windscribe's screen: one round connect button like FLUX, a rounded tile icon, no repeated logo or title,** and the extra actions as rows.
- **The WARP tiles search in «توربو» unless you picked another mode.** The first endpoint that really carries data is used, instead of collecting five and then waiting for a quiet spell: WARP-in-WARP went from 37-47 s to 7-17 s on the phone. The core rescans with a fresh sample when a pass finds nothing, so a hard line is not given up on sooner.
- **Proton: «Ping all servers» is above the server list, with live progress.** While it runs it shows how many servers are done out of all, a progress bar, the way being tried and a Stop button.

### Fixed
- **No more freezes when a test process dies.** A freeze report from Outline's WARP probe showed a deadlock in the bridge to the app's test and carrier processes (`AwgHostClient`): a worker held its lock while waiting for the bind, whose callback arrives on the main thread, and the main thread's own callback for the dead process waited for that lock -- up to 5 s every time. The lock is now held only to set a bind up, never while waiting, and a dead connection is let go off the main thread.
- **The LAN screen no longer says a tunnel is on when none is.** VPN Gate's "connected since" is saved and cleared only by a clean disconnect; after the app was killed or crashed while it was up, the stale value counted as a running tunnel. It now counts only while a VPN of the app is really up.
- **Native crash reports keep what lies past the cut.** A report keeps the top 24,000 characters of a tombstone, and the thread that aborted can come later: the app's libraries and any abort message from the whole tombstone now come first, and the headline is built from them.
- **The operator is named right.** MLM Scout, the Cloudflare Doctor and the strategy engine asked Android for the default SIM -- the calling one on a dual-SIM phone -- and for the network it was registered on, so MCI read as Irancell and back. Now one answer for every screen (`data/Carrier`): the SIM of the cellular network that carries data (Android 11+ names it), else the default data SIM, by that SIM's own operator code.
- **The MLMVPN Quick Settings tile works with every engine.** It knew two of the app's VPN services, so OpenVPN (and Windscribe's OpenVPN family) read as off and a tap connected something else on top. It now sees every tunnel of the app (including the system's own list of VPNs this app owns), stops each engine through its own disconnect so no watchdog brings it back, and brings back an OpenVPN profile, an Amnezia/Proton server and Outline through their own engines (a Proton config replayed raw pointed at a relay port that no longer existed).
- **Proton and Windscribe no longer close the app with LAN sharing on.** The first reports with the new detail (a tester, split tunnel, the smart Iran route and LAN sharing on) showed both crashes in Xray inside the app's own process: with sharing on, a WireGuard server was moved onto Xray for its SOCKS inbound, and stopping that core between attempts panics it now and then -- a Go panic ends the whole app; Proton's Cloudflare carrier started and stopped a core per candidate config in the same process, from the moment the page opened. Now the AmneziaWG core keeps the tunnel in its own process with sharing on, and the share gets the app's own SOCKS port (this app is inside the tunnel); Proton's carrier runs in a process of its own (`:pcarrier`).
- **Proton pings only when asked.** Proton's servers share the store with «آمنزیا», whose screen pings the best servers by itself when it comes into view; that ping took Proton's (and Windscribe's) servers along, and Proton's page showed it as its own -- after a disconnect, say. Automatic pings now leave both sections' servers out, and Proton's page shows progress only for a ping started with its own «پینگ همه».
- **WARP tiles with LAN sharing on no longer give up at 60 s.** With sharing on they take the local proxy path, which waited a flat 60 s; a WireGuard endpoint hunt with nothing pinned took 51 s before the core even validated what it found, and was cut off there. With nothing pinned the wait is now the WARP ladder's budget plus 40 s (110 s for WireGuard); a chosen exit country keeps 60 s.
- **Crash reports that arrive, freezes included.** A tester's Windscribe, Proton and Outline crashes (LAN sharing and split tunnel on) reached the collector only as "CRASH(jvm): crash" with no stack, or not at all. Now: a main thread that stops answering for 4 s while a screen is showing is reported at once, with the code it is stuck in, the breadcrumbs and the memory in use, before anything can kill the app; a native crash or ANR names the screen it happened on ("[windscribe]"), so it is not filed under an older, closed bug; every process saves its breadcrumbs as it goes, so a death that leaves no report still arrives with the trail of screens and connect steps; out of memory, the crash handler gets a reserve back and writes the stack; every report says whether split tunnel, the smart Iran route and LAN sharing were on; a Go abort is named after its library (libgojni.so) instead of libart.so; and each Windscribe connect stage is a breadcrumb. Also: the LAN check no longer reads a VPN's whole route table on the main thread.
- **Windscribe: the tab no longer crashes, and the captcha can be read.** Opening Windscribe after a test could close the app ("Integer cannot be cast to Long" in the results sort, crash #962): the sorts are now written with plain number compares. The login captcha is letters drawn with symbols, so its copy button pasted a jumble; the button is gone and the drawing is shrunk to fit the dialog in black on white. Windscribe login, connect and disconnect now leave breadcrumbs in crash reports. The challenge arrives base64-encoded and was shown undecoded, one long unreadable line; it is now decoded into the drawing itself (rows of `*` forming the characters).
- **MLM Scout no longer crashes on the Psiphon test.** Psiphon's library has its own Go runtime and was started in the app's main process next to Xray's; two Go runtimes in one process abort it (S26 Ultra, 2026-10-10, crash #5). Scout's Psiphon test now runs in a process of its own.
- **Disconnecting Proton no longer freezes the app.** Closing the Stealth relay waited on the main thread for a Cloudflare carrier that was still starting (ANR #844, #845, #848, #866, #880, #896, #897, #898, #944); the carrier is now let go in the background.
- **WireGuard, WARP-in-WARP and MASQUE: the identity no longer shows as "not made yet" after a disconnect.** Between connects the identity is kept only as its sealed (Keystore-encrypted) copy and is restored and reused on the next connect -- it was never deleted -- but the identity row looked for the plain file alone. It now counts the sealed copy too.
- **WireGuard, MASQUE and WARP-in-WARP with Local network sharing on no longer drop every 3 seconds.** In that mode the core publishes a local SOCKS port; when something else already held 127.0.0.1:10808, the core could not open it, its session ended and restarted every 3 s, and the app still said connected because the other listener answered. The core now gets a free port when the usual one is taken.
- **Outline: servers the line drops are tried again from inside Cloudflare WARP.** Out of the public lists only a handful passed the straight test, because none of the Cloudflare routes built for Windscribe, Proton and Amnezia applied to Outline. Now the 60 most promising servers that fail straight are tested again through one shared WARP session, and those that work only that way connect through WARP (one Xray core: Shadowsocks dialled through WARP's WireGuard). A connect also tries WARP when the straight way carries nothing. ECH, PattNG and TLS fragments do not apply: Shadowsocks has no TLS hello to hide or split.
- **A public config that crashes Xray no longer closes the app.** Some public servers drive Xray into a Go panic ("close of closed channel"), which cannot be caught and took the whole app down -- seen on the user's phone in Outline's «test all». Every delay test of the app (`CombineEngine.measureDelay`: config lists, Scout, Arena, emergency, Outline) now runs in its own process (`:xmeasure`); Outline's tests use the `:xprobe` process like Amnezia's, with batches retried in halves when a core dies.
- **Windscribe and Outline through WARP no longer crash the app.** The shared WARP carrier's WireGuard dials through a noise outbound, and that pairing panics Xray now and then ("Unable to update bind ... no outbound handler for dialerProxy", then the panic). The carrier now runs in its own process (`:xcarrier`).
- **Crash reports: deaths by signal are reported.** When the system cannot unwind a native abort (a Go panic, on Android 11) it records the death as SIGNALED rather than CRASH_NATIVE, and those were written down on the phone but never sent -- the Outline crash above was one. SIGNALED (except SIGKILL), EXIT_SELF with an error, DEPENDENCY_DIED and low-memory deaths while carrying the tunnel are now reported too.
- **The three route chips on every method.** Split tunnel, Local network sharing and Smart Iran routing, first on Outline's page, now show on every method's page.
- **MTU for every method.** Settings › Advanced VPN › MTU listed ten methods; Amnezia, Proton, Windscribe, Outline, FLUX, FENIX, MAE, GitHub tunnel, GST, anti-sanction, OpenVPN and Geph had no field and took V2Ray's or Quick Connect's. Each has its own now, defaulting to what it had.
- **Windscribe captcha drawn as a picture, and a wrong answer brings a new one.** The challenge is a black-and-white bitmap written as characters; as text its cells were stretched about 1:2.5 and the letters did not read. It is now drawn as square cells. A wrong answer no longer ends the login with "verification failed": a fresh challenge comes into the same dialog with a note, and a "New code" button asks for another.
- **Amnezia «test all» shows results at once and stops when asked.** Every self-signed Hysteria2 server's certificate was fetched (with a DNS lookup each) before the first result: with 3,000 servers it sat at 0 for minutes, and «Stop» waited on that stage (reproduced on the user's Galaxy A50). Certificates are now fetched batch by batch, Stop clears the progress at once, and batches are 64/48 configs instead of 96/64 (the test process was killed for memory twice on a 4 GB phone).
- **Smart Iran routing no longer heats the phone, and finds Iranian-hosted sites by address.** All ~2,900 ranges as VPN routes overloaded Android's own network stack (whole phone hot, System UI not responding). Only the large ranges are VPN routes now (IPv4 /19+, 207 ranges, 84% of addresses; /16+ before Android 13); the Xray layer still has all of them, and a name in no list is now looked up (`IPIfNonMatch`) so any site whose address is Iranian goes direct. The IPv4/IPv6 counts on its page were shown swapped in Persian.
- **Stopping an Xray core with WireGuard junk no longer panics (likely the "app closes when the VPN stops" crash).** WireGuard's junk packets went through a `dialerProxy` to a freedom outbound with `noises`; when a core stopped, that outbound could close first, WireGuard's bind took its device down while the core closed it too, and Go panicked ("close of closed channel"). Reproduced on the user's A50 at the end of every Amnezia test batch, each then retested in halves. Every core now starts through `NoiseDoor`, which puts the junk on a loopback `dokodemo-door` instead (same bytes on the wire); test processes also end themselves on stop, and a test's results are judged before the stop. Measured after: 260 servers a minute (64 before), no panics.
- **MLM Scout: IPv4/IPv6, UDP, the line's delay and its speed read right.** IPv4/IPv6 were judged by a TLS handshake to one.one.one.one and dns.google, both filtered by name in Iran, so both read "blocked" where online tests said open; they are now a TCP handshake to many places of each family. The line's delay and the UDP verdict's TCP comparison no longer depend on 1.1.1.1 alone, ICMP tries three targets, and the direct speed falls back to Cloudflare's and CacheFly's files (Google's download host refuses Iranian addresses). The details page showed "WORKS" in English.
- **MLM Scout: WARP, MASQUE, WireGuard and WARP-in-WARP start where the tile starts.** Each test ran in a fresh, empty directory: no last working endpoint, no endpoint cache, so every variant began a full endpoint hunt -- minutes each, and «این دور جواب نداد» when it ran out of time, while the tile reconnects in two seconds. A run now starts from what Scout last found for that method and IP family (else the tile's own `tunnel-lastconn.toml` and gateway cache, same family only) and keeps what it finds.
- **MLM Scout: Tor as two results, as on its tile.** «Bridges» (automatic bridges from every source, automatic exit country) and «Base» (WebTunnel, meek, Snowflake, obfs4 in turn); each says which way connected, or which ways were tried.
- **MLM Scout's scan is shorter and its verdicts truer.** Measured on the user's A50 (a scan took ~61 min): the tunnel-core methods and Tor no longer wait for WARP's lane (they share nothing with it); country exits are one gateway per country (the tile also dials one), and a fixed peer gets 60 s instead of 210 s; the quick pass tries the IP family that last worked (else IPv6 where present -- on that Wi-Fi WireGuard/WARP-in-WARP pass only over IPv6); MASQUE runs every HTTP/3 way (direct, then each country) before HTTP/2, and a country's or family's HTTP/2 is skipped once its HTTP/3 carried data, as are Psiphon's and Outline's WARP chains once the plain way works. Amnezia's pick prefers a foreign exit (the fastest server was inside Iran: «خروجی‌اش ایران است»); Outline gets an «inside WARP» variant like its section; the free configs get 240 s (one scan ran out of 100 s while the method worked). Psiphon climbs the tile's own ladder (CDN-fronted → direct → peer relay) from the rung that last carried the tile, all rungs in one process (a process per rung was restarted by the system and killed mid-rung), and says which rung connected -- it found nothing in 150 s with the bare config and now connects via the fronted rung in ~13 s. Amnezia no longer requires a foreign exit: many public WireGuard servers are WARP, whose exits Cloudflare places in the user's own country, so a working one read «خروجی‌اش ایران است» with YouTube open. A fixed country peer gets 35 s (working ones answer in 6-13 s) and MASQUE's direct HTTP/2 75 s. Measured end to end on the A50: 61 min → 31 min.
- **MLM Scout no longer brings a working method up a second time for its speed.** The speed sample is taken on the first connection (one download at a time across methods side by side); the separate speed round only covers what was not sampled. The line's own direct speed is now measured even when no method needs a speed round (it stayed on «…»).
- **The Local network screen sees every engine's tunnel.** Only Settings' LAN sharing had been wired to all methods; the Local network screen and its tile said "No tunnel is running" with WARP's tile, Geph, Tor, Psiphon or an AmneziaWG-only server up. A tunnel now counts as up when the tunnel service has published a SOCKS port, or when the system reports a VPN owned by this app.
- **The WireGuard tab no longer freezes on open.** It asked the Keystore to open the WARP identity relay's key while drawing (ANR #870); it now only checks that the key is stored.
- **Domain fronting's setup could erase the other configs.** Setup added its config and saved before the stored list had been read in (the read is asynchronous), so on a cold start the saved list held only the fronting config and the built-ins; seen on the phone. Setup now waits for the list first.
- **Domain fronting connects again.** The live check a server-less config must pass (since 1.2.40) probed through the app's own trust, which does not take the phone's fronting certificate, so domain fronting was refused at every connect. The check now accepts that certificate (its key never leaves the phone). Measured on the user's home Wi-Fi (2026-10-10), where every Serverless Iran config opens nothing -- the filter now puts a handshake cut into TCP pieces back together, drops one cut into TLS records (even for google.com) and blocks their DNS -- domain fronting opened YouTube, its video servers, Google, Instagram and Reddit. Its two TLS terminators also stopped listening on every interface (anyone on the same Wi-Fi could reach them) and now listen on loopback only.
- **OpenVPN now follows the app-wide split tunnel and LAN sharing** (the OpenVPN tile and Windscribe's OpenVPN family): the apps chosen in Settings are included or left out, and while the tunnel is up the app's Local Port is served (password and blocked devices respected), so other devices on the hotspot can use it. Direct WireGuard (Amnezia, Proton, Windscribe) follows the split tunnel too.
- **FLUX tries every way in on every connect, the way Defyx does.** On the user's Wi-Fi (2026-10-08) Cloudflare answered over IPv6 but not IPv4, yet FLUX spent all its rounds on direct foreign servers the line had cut (120 tries, none reached), and the Cloudflare servers it did reach were reset at their server name. Now every round mixes the methods: direct servers, Cloudflare servers plain, with ECH (the name encrypted under cloudflare-ech.com, the key fetched on the real line over DNS or DNS-over-HTTPS where UDP is dropped), with PattNG's cut hello and with the fragment splits -- and the user's own Cloudflare configs (panels, scanner combinations: the handshake the Doctor proved on that line) join the race first, on their clean address even where random edges are cut. The method that wins is remembered per network and tried first next time. Four rounds instead of three. Untested on a phone.
- **Google Apps Script: "wrong password", 404, and "healthy but no data" fixed.** A Deployment ID pasted as the whole web-app URL is now cut down to the ID (it used to make Google answer 404), and a 404 says plainly that the ID is wrong or the deployment was archived. The password is cleaned the same way for the script, the test and the saved relay (Persian digits, a trailing space), so the three no longer disagree. A script error or a used-up daily quota no longer looks like a wrong password: the script now answers with the real error, and the quota gets its own message. The core takes one password for every relay, so relays with a different password were silently sent the wrong one; the tunnel now uses only the relays sharing one password and the test marks the others «رمز متفاوت». The tunnel used to dial a random ticked Google IP and cover name -- two of which (googlevideo.com, ytimg.com) have no certificate on Google's servers and several IPs time out on some lines; it now measures the ticked pairs the way the tunnel uses them and takes the fastest that answers, and the relay test says «مسیر بسته» when none does. Untested on a phone.
- **Crash sweep of 1.2.40 (every reported crash looked at).** Out of memory: the scanner groups lived in one JSON string in the app's preferences -- with tens of thousands of configs a 40+ MB string kept in memory all the time and rebuilt at every save -- they now stream to a file of their own; a huge file picked by mistake in «add config» is refused instead of read whole. Freezes: sealing the WARP identities at app start and after a stop, the home wallpaper's blur, and adding a large subscription no longer block the screen.
