Crashes: the exit-country ping with «WARP»'s sealed identity, FENIX's ping list, AmneziaWG on Android 7 (java.time), copying thousands of configs, a system restart while a service starts, a ROM refusing the VPN notification, a busy port in Amnezia's test, links with no browser installed. All crash issues are closed; any that comes back on 1.2.41 reopens by itself.
- **The build no longer needs Google Maven for desugaring.** The desugaring library is kept in the repository (`local-maven`), checked against the published SHA-1, because Google Maven answers 404 from Iran.
- **MAE no longer drops every app's connection about once a minute.** Each app check that finished, each rotated WARP endpoint and each newly found config rebuilt the whole tunnel, and every app saw it (TikTok: "disconnected / connected" every minute, on one Wi-Fi). Now a change the running tunnel can carry is switched live; the rest wait for the checks to finish and come at most every 2 minutes (apps added or their sites learned) or every 15 minutes (a better route that no app needs right now). A dead route and the user's own "didn't open" are still fixed at once. On the phone: 4 rebuilds in 4 minutes before, one batched rebuild in 11 minutes with a full re-check after; none in steady use.
- **A VPN reconnected whenever mobile data came and went behind a working Wi-Fi.** The service watched every network with internet; it now follows only the network the tunnel really rides.
- **WireGuard made its identity and then never connected.** The tunnel core's WireGuard search read its mode and address family from environment variables the app never set, so it always searched IPv4 alone, slowly, whatever the settings said -- and on a line where WARP over IPv4 is closed (0 of 2,540 endpoints on the phone, IPv6 within seconds) it never found one. The app now hands the core the right values, and WireGuard always searches afresh (its shortcut to the last endpoint fell back to IPv4 alone when that endpoint died). On the phone: connected in 4 s, 5 MB in 1.5 s.
- **The WARP tiles sometimes searched IPv4 only although the line has IPv6.** A check made on the screen's thread could not open its test connection, read as "no IPv6" and was remembered for a minute; such a check is no longer remembered.
- **Proton VPN no longer glitches, drops or slows down after a few minutes.** A free Cloudflare Worker cuts a long connection after about 10 MB, and each cut stalled the tunnel and sometimes made it reconnect (with other lamps lighting up). Proton now keeps a second connection ready and moves onto it before the cut, notices a stalled connection within 3-5 s and moves on without reconnecting, and a way that keeps stalling is replaced by another way that works on that internet -- and the tunnel goes back to the fastest way once it works again. A way that works for a while and is then cut is no longer chosen again on that internet.
- **Cloudflare scanner: the health ping showed no combined config connected.** The configs made by «ترکیب» now carry the method the scan proved on this internet; on the phone 20 of 20 combined configs passed the health ping and a real connection carried data.
- **The home lamp no longer jumps to another engine while Proton is connecting.**
- **Amnezia: the list had pushed out almost every WireGuard server.** The pool of 3000 was filled by value, and the Hysteria2 lists -- thousands of servers, three in four of which need certificate checks turned off, which this engine never does -- outranked the WireGuard lists whose servers had failed over the blocked line: 34 WireGuard servers were left. Every plain WireGuard server is now kept, «test the best» always includes the WireGuard servers that work or are untried, and their old line-only failures are forgotten once so they are tried again both ways.
- **Amnezia: Hysteria2 servers with a self-signed certificate can be used.** Three in four public Hysteria2 links ask for certificate checks to be turned off (`insecure=1`), which this core no longer allows, so not one of them could ever pass a test. The app now reads the certificate each such server shows -- a short QUIC handshake of its own, through salamander obfuscation too -- and pins exactly that certificate (`pinnedPeerCertSha256`): checks stay on, and only that server's own certificate is accepted. It is read again before every test and every connect, so a server that made itself a new certificate is followed; a link's own `pinSHA256` is kept too. Servers removed earlier for this come back on the next refresh. On the phone (home Wi-Fi): a self-signed server that always failed passed the test in 439 ms and connected, exit Hong Kong. Most of these servers are still unreachable on a line that drops UDP to them (2-3 of 150 answered on that line).
- **The release build failed** on missing texts of the GitHub tunnel.
- **Proton VPN: the sign-in page stayed white.** The page loaded completely but was drawn above the screen: the in-app WebView was left WRAP_CONTENT high, which makes Android give the page a viewport height of 0 (CSS `100vh` = 0), and Proton's full-height layout pushed the whole form above the top edge. The WebView now fills its box; FENIX's two sign-in pages had the same setup and get the same fix. Measured on a phone through DevTools before and after.
- **Proton VPN: signing in now brings the servers by itself.** Signing in and pressing Done gave nothing unless the user also went through Proton's WireGuard dashboard by hand, server by server. Now, once signed in, the app takes the account's free servers itself with the calls Proton's own dashboard makes (WebClients, WireGuardConfigurationSection): the plan's tier, the server list (within the tier, no Secure Core or Tor, best score first) and one key made on the phone and registered as one WireGuard configuration of the account. That one key serves every server (measured: a server's key carried data on two others), so EVERY free server the account has is taken -- 111 on 2026-10-06, in 10 countries -- with one entry in Proton's dashboard; a key near its expiry (they last a year) is replaced and every config made again. «Add more servers» takes only the ones not yet kept. The password still never reaches the app: the page's own session is used, the newest one when several sign-ins left cookies (an older one is refused with "insufficient scope"). Done also takes them if that has not happened yet. On a phone: 111 servers seconds after signing in. The key is unit-tested against RFC 8032 and against the X25519 key WireGuard derives from it.
- **Proton VPN: the page now survives a slow or flaky way in.** The check that Proton answers through the engine gets three tries of 20 s (requests through FLUX took 5-17 s on a busy line, and one 7 s try sent users back to the engine list), FLUX counts as ready only after its own real-traffic check, the page reloads itself twice when a first load is lost, the account requests are sent again when the route cuts them, and an engine that drops for a moment no longer throws the open page away (a line says so, and it carries on when the engine is back).
- **Proton VPN connects where Proton is filtered: Stealth, over IPv6 first.** Measured on the user's line with a WireGuard client written for the test (and checked first on an unfiltered network): to most of Proton's IPv4 addresses the WireGuard handshake is answered once and then the filter blackholes the connection, over UDP on every port and over TLS alike, whatever the server name. The same servers' IPv6 addresses, and some IPv4 ranges, carry data. So every Proton server is now reached the way Proton's own apps reach it where UDP is filtered -- Stealth, WireGuard inside TLS on TCP 443 with TunSafe framing (from ProtonVPN/wireguard-go), a made-up server name -- through a local relay the Amnezia engine is pointed at, IPv6 first and IPv4 after, with a fresh connection whenever a handshake goes unanswered on a silent one. Each server's IPv6 address comes from Proton's server list. Proton servers are tested with a longer wait (12 s, 32 at a time: the first request also opens the TLS connection) and the list is sorted fastest first. On a phone: 44 of 111 servers answered on that line (Netherlands about 410 ms, US, Romania, Poland, Canada); connected to Canada and to the Netherlands, and a site filtered on that line opened through it.
- **Proton VPN: the home lamp was on the Amnezia tile.** Proton's servers are connected by the Amnezia engine, so its tile lit; it now lights Proton's own when the server is Proton's.
- **Proton VPN: a failed connect was invisible.** When none of the account's servers answered (a network that blocks UDP), the screen still said "ready to connect"; it now says that no Proton server answered and that this internet may be blocking UDP.
- **FLUX kept disconnecting and reconnecting.** The race stopped 250 ms after the first route answered, so on a slow line the tunnel almost always had one route and no standby for its balancer to move to: every dead route meant a whole new search and a tunnel restart, and a search that found nothing turned the VPN off. The race now keeps up to three routes (up to 1.5 s more, ending as soon as three answered); a health check gives the route two tries of 10 s before it counts as failed; and a failover keeps the tunnel and "connected" while the replacement is raced, swaps only to a route that answered (not at all if the current one answers again meanwhile), and when nothing is found tries twice more, 30 s apart, before giving up.
- **Game Booster: Amnezia and FENIX were cut mid-game.** The monitor took a healthy Amnezia or FENIX path for a lost one and shut it down after a few rounds; every path is now seen by its own id and health check. Stopping while connecting now really cancels the connection in progress, and a measurement no longer switches off WARP or another connection.
- **Game Booster: more honest measurements.** Packet reordering, which always read zero, is counted correctly; cancelling a measurement cleans it up; and on a network where the test target is closed directly, the tunnel race still runs.

## [1.2.40] — 2026-10-03

### Added
- **76 real ways to reach Cloudflare, and XHTTP over HTTP/2.** A second, non-Google DNS; a cut inside the server name; the ClientHello in two TLS records; a mixed-case server name; TLS 1.3; three Cloudflare address ranges; iOS, Edge and Android fingerprints; more ports; ECH on clean and proven addresses; the WARP identity through your worker without splitting; and the config exactly as written. For a config whose server speaks XHTTP: HTTP/2 or HTTP/1.1, packet-up or stream-up, with or without fragmentation (never on a WebSocket config, never stream-up on a Worker). Every one is applied by the runtime and validated with real data; each is chosen and saved per network and stage only after that.
- **XHTTP configs from the MLM panel.** A new «دریافت کانفیگ xhttp» row above «کاربران». Panel build 23 hands every request of an XHTTP packet-up session to that session's Durable Object (the existing SessionDO class, so no new migration), so the uploads and the download finally meet on workers.dev — the reason XHTTP was paused on 2026-09-26. The config is made from the app's own panel user, put through the Cloudflare engine's CONNECT stage on this network (HTTP 204, HTTPS, an exact download, an echoed upload) and added only if that passes. Panels installed earlier need «به‌روزرسانی» first.
- **Cloudflare Doctor inside Cloud.** Five setup steps cover six independent stages, with strategies learned per network and stage. Only a validated strategy is reused; a fresh runtime failure revokes it. When the account route proves an unsaved credential, Doctor asks before adding it to the sealed account store. Tunnel verification includes HTTP 204, HTTPS, a 64 KiB download and an acknowledged 16 KiB upload. Developer details unlock after seven taps on the About version. Retained diagnostic credentials and native identities are encrypted; native TOML files are materialized only for core execution and cleaned up on teardown.
- **Doctor deploy choices.** Once the Deploy route is proven, Doctor shows the available panels (BPB, EDG, Nahan, Spider, Netra, Gozargah, Nova and MLM). The user can select one, several, or none; only the selected panels are deployed, and each result is shown separately so a partial failure does not hide successful panels.
- **Doctor config choices.** Once the Config route is proven, Doctor lists the installed panels and lets the user fetch configs from one, several, or none. Each successful panel is filed as its own Cloud group; partial failures are reported separately.
- **Adding an account, deploying and fetching configs now work where Cloudflare is filtered by name.** Many people could not even connect their Cloudflare account, deploy a worker or fetch a config, because on their network the names api.cloudflare.com and workers.dev are read in the first TLS packet and dropped. The app's own Cloudflare calls (adding and verifying an account, creating the subdomain, uploading a worker, KV and D1, fetching configs from the panels, and updating workers) now take the same route the rest of the app uses for Cloudflare: a Cloudflare edge address that answers on this network, with the first TLS handshake (the ClientHello) sent in small pieces so the server name is not in one packet for the filter to match. It is the same thing the app already did to fetch a WARP key, now on the account, deploy and config paths too. Where Cloudflare was reachable already, nothing changes. No new library, no increase in app size.
- **Exit country for «وارپ», MASQUE and WireGuard.** A new «کشور خروجی» row on all three screens opens a list of countries with flags and Persian names. The tunnel then comes out in that country instead of Iran.
  - Works through public NAT64 gateways: the Cloudflare endpoint is dialled as `[<gateway prefix>:<Cloudflare IPv4>]:<port>`, so Cloudflare takes the connection in the gateway's country. Needs IPv6 on the user's line; without it the app says so instead of failing silently.
  - Checked on 2026-10-03 with the Aether core, WireGuard and MASQUE both: Netherlands, United Kingdom, Germany (2 gateways), Finland (2), United States and Hong Kong carried WARP end to end. Slovenia, Australia, Lithuania and a few more listed gateways are kept; the ping shows whether they answer.
  - **Ping every country** at the top of the list: a short real connection through each gateway, 4 at a time, reporting ping, speed and the exit country Cloudflare really gives.
  - **Direct connection** stays the default and is explained: an Iranian IP is shown, but filtered sites open. **Fastest country** picks the gateway the last ping measured fastest.
  - If a gateway does not come up (30 s for «وارپ», 45 s for MASQUE/WireGuard), the same country's next gateway is tried automatically; gateways that fail move to the back of the line.
  - Changing the country while connected reconnects right away.
  - The list is refreshed daily from nat64.xyz and the BPB panel list, de-duplicated by the 96 bits of each prefix; a user can add their own NAT64 gateway. Holding a country copies a WireGuard config that exits there.

- **FENIX, a new home tile: Firefox's free VPN on the user's own Firefox account.** Mozilla's Fastly servers in 33 countries, 50 GB a month, on the app's own Xray core.
  - Sign in once (email, password, emailed code). Only an encrypted sign-in key is kept; the password is never stored. **Create a free Firefox account** opens Mozilla's own sign-up page inside the app. Mozilla's occasional browser check opens in the app for the user to pass; the app does not imitate a browser.
  - Fast: every connection is a CONNECT stream on one shared HTTP/2 session, dialled by address, with a standby path Xray switches to on its own; HTTP/1.1 is the automatic fallback.
  - No leaks: DNS inside the tunnel (FakeDNS + DoH through it), only LAN direct, QUIC refused, other UDP dropped inside, no IPv6 on the TUN.
  - The short-lived pass is renewed ahead of expiry; the renewal (a ~1 s core restart) waits for the screen to be off, or runs 2 minutes before expiry at the latest.
  - Countries with flags and Persian names, **Ping every country** (a real TLS handshake each), **Fastest country**, **Mozilla's pick**, and the data left this month.
  - A new MAE foreign exit, proven per app like the others (switch on the FENIX screen).
  - **Several accounts.** Add as many Firefox accounts as you like. Each connection takes the next account in turn, checked first to really work and have data left; if an account runs out while connected, the tunnel moves to the next by itself. The Accounts page shows each account's email, data left and refill date. The 50 GB limit is monthly. One button, Sign in or create an account: Mozilla's own page opens in the app, you sign in or sign up with email or Continue with Google, and the account is added on its own at the end; once per account, and the password never passes through the app.
  - Checked from an Iranian line on 2026-10-03: sign-in, Guardian and the server list answer directly; Xray reaches the servers over HTTP/2 and TLS 1.3. A full connection still needs a real account on a phone.

- **Store: downloads pause and resume.** Downloads can be paused and resumed and continue where they stopped, not from the start. If the connection drops mid-download, it retries three times from the same point before trying the next mirror. Progress reads “12 of 45 MB · 1.2 MB/s · 20 s left”, and Cancel download is separate.

- **Tor: bridge management.** A new Bridges row on Tor's screen, beside Connection mode and Exit country. Automatic takes the best tested bridges from Tor's official service (Moat) and three bridge collectors, drops duplicates and dead ones, refreshes itself every few hours, and ranks first the bridges Tor really connected through. The list shows country (flag and name), kind and “Working · 187 ms”, with single and bulk tests, sorting, and manual bridges with clear errors; plus new WebTunnel and plain-bridge modes. About 260 working bridges ship with the app for the first run; the list grows with every refresh; bridges are grouped by country; “Connect through this bridge” picks one by hand; and “Remove down bridges” clears the dead ones in one tap. Once Tor connects, the bridge's country (the way in) is shown beside the exit country, with a word on why they can differ and how to pick an exit country or the fastest bridge.

- **Real validation for each Cloudflare stage.** The deploy test creates a nonce Worker and deletes it. Config, scan and connect require real user resources and verified data transfer. Missing prerequisites are reported honestly; legacy diagnostics are available in developer mode.
- **Steadier Cloudflare connections in every region.** The Doctor reports showed some Cloudflare addresses working in one Iranian region and dead in the next, and the phone's DNS sometimes handing out a dead or bogus one. The app now checks a few Cloudflare addresses per network in about three seconds and uses one that answers -- for the Cloudflare account, the panels, WARP registration, and configs whose address does not answer on this network (same worker, another address). Addresses the scanner finds join that list.

- **Aether 2.3.0, and HTTP/3 or HTTP/2 chosen for you.** MASQUE and gool now pick their carrier on their own: HTTP/3 first, then HTTP/2, then HTTP/2 with the ClientHello split (Aether's `--fragment`, its default sizes, the developer's advice for Irancell-type lines). The one that really carried data is remembered for that network and used first next time, with no testing; if it stops working, it is forgotten and the others are tried again. gool is back on the Aether screen: since 2.3.0 its WireGuard hop rides inside MASQUE and comes out at a foreign address.
- **Store: Iran configs follow @patterniha's repository.** The item used to come only from the app's own channel, so v51 and v52 upstream were never noticed. The store now reads `Serverless-frag{A,B}.jsonc` from patterniha/Serverless-for-Iran (jsDelivr when GitHub is filtered), rebuilds the nineteen rows on the phone with the same checks as the desktop generator (server-less outbounds only; a moved shape stops the install), has the real Xray core start three of them, and only then installs. Rollback keeps the previous list.

- **Game Booster: filtered or sanctioned games, and three more routes.** A new switch under the game, «This game is filtered, sanctioned or won't load»: with it on (or after «can't connect» / «won't update» in «Having trouble?») the direct line is never used for that game. Instead each tunnel that leaves the filter -- Amnezia, WireGuard (with the exit country chosen on its screen), WARP and FENIX -- is timed to the game region's own server (a full TLS handshake through it, the same method for each) and the fastest is applied. WireGuard, Amnezia and FENIX can also be pinned in Advanced. MASQUE wears a red «قطع» tag on the home screen.

### Changed
- **Store: a core update that would break the app is refused before it is installed.** The Aether and tunnel cores now list the names the app drives them by (environment variables, config fields, the log line it waits for); a new version missing any of them is not installed, and the working copy stays.

### Fixed
- **SNI anti-filter carried nothing.** On the new filtering every one of the engine's 14 methods carried 0 bytes, even the one that splits the server name itself across two packets: the filter reassembles. SNI now has a 15th method, ECH, tried second: the same config goes straight to a Cloudflare edge with its server name encrypted. Measured on the phone: 9 of 9 SNI configs carried data and a browser opened pages abroad through it. The method that worked is remembered. Also: the builder now sees configs fetched in Cloud (it said «nothing to convert» right after a BPB fetch), SNI configs are no longer reshaped by the Cloudflare optimizer (which could swap the front's address for an edge IP), and their ClientHello is kept small for the front's splitting.
- **«وارپ در وارپ» dropped about a minute after connecting; «ماسک» never tried HTTP/3.** The built-in tunnel core is rebuilt from its newer source: the crash on tearing down WARP-in-WARP («JoinHandle polled after completion») is gone, MASQUE also tries UDP ports 1701, 8095, 500 and 4500 before falling back to HTTP/2, and a WireGuard tunnel is no longer declared dead after 10 quiet seconds (120 s, as on the Aether screen). «ماسک»'s transport gets «خودکار» as default (a fixed HTTP/2 left over from a test is moved to it once): HTTP/3 first, then HTTP/2 with fragmentation, and the one that connected is remembered for that network.
- **MLM XHTTP carried nothing.** The MLM panel's deployer never sent the Durable Object binding XHTTP needs on workers.dev (only Config Studio's did). It does now, with the same fallbacks; panel build 24 so every MLM panel is offered the update.
- **Store kept showing a check made by the previous app build** (newest Iran configs «50» while upstream had 52). A check from another build is now redone when the store opens. The app also ships v52's two configs, and a Store update of the Iran configs only adds the new version's configs at the top; existing ones are never touched. The Iran configs test runs four configs at a time.
- **Iran configs: «تست» marked working configs as dead and dead ones as working.** It only sent a plain request to each config's DNS server, which says nothing about the config: the original rows reach their DNS through their own fragmenting, so the bare request was filtered. Every config is now run for real and asked to open YouTube through itself: green opened it, orange connects but cannot open filtered sites, red opened nothing; rows fill in one by one. Connecting one of them now also has to open a site before it is shown as connected.
- **Cloudflare Doctor and the connection engine, audited end to end.** A connect no longer runs a full diagnosis first (minutes): the proven way is applied at once and the live tunnel's own data check validates it; only when no data moves is a bounded search run and the connect retried once — a tunnel that starts and carries nothing is FAILED, and a slow check can no longer leave the core running behind a stuck «connecting». The data check no longer depends on one third-party site. The scanner uses the user's own address list and ports again (an optimization could replace them with a few known edges or drop every IPv6 address), measures in parallel again (it had been cut to one test at a time), and skips the plain-TLS pre-filter when a shaped handshake is what works here. A rejected credential stops discovery at once and is never held against a route; a bare 403 (WAF or captive page) is no longer reported as a wrong token. Learning is kept across a flaky DNS check and across Wi-Fi reconnects. A deploy no longer creates a test worker every time. Whole-config engines (MAE, FLUX, FENIX) are no longer reshaped.
- **Doctor's screens.** Rebuilt from the app's own iOS pieces (glass cards, grouped rows, iOS alerts), in Persian and English. The first-run check never blocks Cloud for someone without an account, a step is green only after real validation, and «بعد از افزودن حساب» / «بعد از افزودن کانفیگ» marks what cannot be checked yet. Strategy details stay in developer mode (seven taps on the About version toggle it).
- **Cloudflare connectivity optimization, on by default.** Strategies are applied only after validation for the current network and stage. A fresh failure revokes the saved winner; explicit link settings remain supported.
- **Stability: reported crashes fixed.** From the automatic crash reports: the crash when the app was in the background and the network dropped and came back (half of all reports), Android 7 crashes, the Config Studio install crash on a weak connection, bulk tests crashing old phones, freezes at app start and on Tor's screen, the home screen crashing on a very large wallpaper photo, and several smaller crashes.
- **Store: no version shown for Xray, Psiphon and AmneziaWG.** The version in use and the developer's newest on its own GitHub (XTLS, Psiphon, AmneziaWG) are now shown. These three are built into the app and update with it. AmneziaWG's source now points at the library the app actually uses.
- **Geph: the country test results vanished after connecting.** The last run now stays, with every country and its numbers, says how long ago it was measured, and offers Measure again.
- **Geph: the country test said “no answer” for every country.** Each country had only 30 s (the real connection has 120 s) and was tested over a different path from the real connection. Now each gets up to 90 s and is checked exactly as the real connection is, a failed download no longer marks a working country as dead, and a country that fails says why. The speed test file changed too: through Geph's exits Google answered the old one with 404, so the speed figure meant nothing.
- **Geph: connects without making an account by hand.** Connect used to say “make an account first”. Now, with no account, the app makes a free Geph account itself (no e-mail or password; first time only, usually a minute or two) and then connects. The account code is on Geph's account page as before.
- **FENIX: data left never moved.** “50 of 50 GB” was only read when connecting. It now refreshes when the screen opens, every 3 minutes while connected and from the refresh button, and shows the refill date.

## [1.2.39] — 2026-10-01

### Added
- **«آمنزیا» (Amnezia), a new home tile.**
  - Free WireGuard servers, connected through AmneziaWG with junk packets in front of the handshake.
  - `wireguard://` links and Hysteria2 servers from 11 public GitHub lists, with a jsDelivr mirror when GitHub is filtered.
  - The user's own `.conf` / `.zip` files, clipboard paste, and «Open with» from Telegram.
  - 135 servers that were working when the app was built ship inside it, so the section works before the first download.
  - Real delay like v2ray: a real request through every server, from batched probe cores in their own `:xprobe` process, so a Go panic in one server cannot take the app down. Plain WireGuard is tested with the same junk packets it connects with; servers only AmneziaWG can speak are tested in `:awgprobe`.
  - Results are learned per network; the most promising servers are tested first; dead servers (3 failures in a row) are removed; refreshed lists bring new ones.
  - Countries come from the list or the name first, then from Cloudflare's trace through the server and through the tunnel, which moves a server to the country it really exits in. WARP accounts have a group of their own.
  - Connect shows each step until a real request has gone through the tunnel, and can be cancelled at any time. With fallback on, the next best server of the same country is tried.
  - iOS-style design: a main page with the connect button and the chosen location, and a separate server list with search, protocol filter and country chips. Swipe-to-delete needs a deliberate swipe, and every delete can be undone.
  - A WireGuard file that cannot be used now says exactly why (no [Peer] section, no Endpoint, a bad key, no Address). UTF-16 files are read too.
- **«دکتر کلادفلر» (Cloudflare Doctor), in Tools and in account troubleshooting.** One run checks four things separately and says where each one breaks: the Cloudflare account, getting configs from panels, connecting configs, and why the IP scanner finds nothing.
  - Plain results on screen: "problems found", "what works on this internet", and notes about the line that do not stop the user's own things from working.
  - The run keeps going when the user leaves the app. The report is a privacy-filtered text file to share in the mlmvpn group.
  - The test plan can be updated from the signed Store channel without a new APK.
  - Account checks come from the app's own check, so a working account is not reported as rejected; config tests run crash-isolated and also while a VPN is up.
- **FLUX — MLM FLUX Engine, a new engine of its own.** Three controls: a country (or Automatic), IPv4 / IPv6 / Both, and Connect. FLUX picks the protocol, the node, the Cloudflare edge, the address family, fragmenting and the standby routes itself, on the app's existing Xray core.
  - **Works where Cloudflare is cut.** On each network FLUX measures, in about 1.5 s, whether Cloudflare's edges are reachable. Where they are not, Cloudflare-fronted nodes are left out of the race entirely, and only direct routes are tried: VLESS REALITY, Trojan/VLESS TLS and Hysteria2 over UDP. Where Cloudflare works, each CDN-fronted node is tried over several edges, and the edges that answer are remembered for that network.
  - **Its own sources**, separate from Free Configs and Quick Connect: Free-Configs (Cloudflare-fronted, tested from Iran by its publisher) and filtered REALITY, Hysteria2 and Trojan lists. Lists are cached and refreshed in the background with ETag, and a user can add their own subscription.
  - **Fast first connect.** On a network FLUX has seen, it connects to the route that worked there at once, without racing. Otherwise a two-stage race runs:
    - stage 1: TCP plus a TLS handshake with each node's own SNI;
    - stage 2: a real request through every survivor in one probe core, with an explicit node-to-port map.

    The best answer within a 250 ms grace window wins, not merely the first.
  - **Stable.** The tunnel carries the winner and two standbys on other servers behind one balancer, watched by Xray's burst observatory. A dead primary is replaced in under a second, without restarting the VPN. Health checks run with the screen on and stretch from 1 to 5 minutes while the route is healthy. A failing route is benched by a circuit breaker. A new network gets its own route.
  - **No leaks.** All DNS is answered inside the tunnel (FakeDNS, with DoH through the tunnel behind it). Servers are dialled by IP. In IPv4 mode the VPN comes up without IPv6, so Android blocks the family instead of letting it out. Plaintext VLESS and VLESS/Trojan with certificate checks off are refused. Self-signed Hysteria2 is tried only when nothing verified works.
  - **"Connected" means connected.** It is shown only after a real request through the running tunnel succeeded.
  - **The country is measured, not read from the node's name.** The exit is asked through the route itself, from two independent sources, and the proof expires after 24 h. The country list shows only countries with a proven exit, and marks those not yet checked on the current network.
  - Memory is per network: the best route on MCI and on Irancell are kept apart, and changing the country forgets nothing.
  - A hidden diagnostics page opens on a long press of the FLUX title.
- **MAE can use FLUX.** FLUX's proven routes on the current network join MAE's foreign exits. They are read from FLUX's store only: FLUX never probes or starts a tunnel for MAE.

### Changed
- **AmneziaWG runs in its own `:awg` process**, so it never shares a process with Xray's Go runtime and switching between them no longer needs an app restart. WARP Server 2 uses the same path. A failed AmneziaWG start now reports FAILED at once.
- **OpenVPN accepts free `.ovpn` files.** Unknown or unused options (inactive, setenv, fast-io, push-peer-info, ...) and unknown inline blocks are dropped instead of refusing the file; scripts and plugins are dropped and never run. Profiles that carry their own username and password, or need none, connect without a TunnelBear account. A file with no display name or extension is still read, and a failed import shows the real reason.
- The Quick Settings tile reconnects FLUX through FLUX itself, with this network's route. After a network drop, the VPN service asks FLUX for the new network's route instead of replaying the old one.
- **FLUX: up to three rounds per connect, each on fresh candidates.** A round that finds nothing, or whose winner fails inside the tunnel, moves on to nodes not tried yet instead of measuring the same ones again.
- **FLUX: detailed logcat under the `FLUX` tag** (`adb logcat -s FLUX`), with no credentials. It covers:
  - each source and what it yielded;
  - the network verdict, per Cloudflare edge;
  - every candidate's stage-1 and stage-2 result with its reason;
  - the race ranking;
  - the tunnel start and the service's answer;
  - every canary and health check;
  - network changes;
  - the final failure reason.
- **FLUX: country and IP version can be changed while connected.** The tunnel moves to a route for the new choice.
- **FLUX: more countries in the list.**
  - Countries claimed by node names are listed and marked "not verified yet". Choosing one races those nodes first, and FLUX still accepts only an exit measured in that country.
  - After a Wi-Fi connect, FLUX measures where up to 24 untried nodes exit, at most once in 6 h per network, so verified countries accumulate.
  - When the country sources disagree, a fourth one (ifconfig.co) breaks the tie.

### Fixed
- **FLUX: "no working route" although the race had found one.** With a single winning route, the tunnel config carried a `leastLoad` balancer without the burst observatory it depends on, and the core refused to start ("not all dependencies are resolved"). A single route now goes out directly. With standbys, the balancer and the observatory always come together. If the core still refuses the full config, FLUX retries at once with the primary route alone.
- **FLUX: one bad node no longer sinks a whole race.** This core has removed `allowInsecure`, so one self-signed Hysteria2 node made the probe core refuse to start, and every candidate in that round was lost. Self-signed links are now rejected like the others. If the probe core still refuses a batch, the candidates that do build are found and raced.
- **FLUX: no 15-second wait on a failed start.** FLUX moves on as soon as the VPN service gives up.
- **FLUX: IPv6 mode said IPv6 did not work, on a network where it does.** The Cloudflare check sampled random addresses across whole IPv6 /32s, most of them unrouted, so it judged Cloudflare-over-IPv6 cut and left every CDN-fronted node out. It now uses the 16 /48 prefixes the app measured serving from Iran (`CfFamily.V6_PREFIXES`). Verdicts taken with the old sampler are measured again once.
- **FLUX: servers whose names Iran's resolver poisons.** Such names resolve to the block page, which over IPv6 is `2001:4188:2:600::/64`. These answers are now recognised, and the name is resolved through DoH by IP instead, which also brings in the IPv6 addresses an IPv6 race needs.
- **FLUX: an honest IPv6 message.** "This network has no IPv6" is shown only when it has none. Otherwise the message is "no server answered over IPv6 right now".
- **FLUX: "FLUX" was shown twice on its page.**

## [1.2.38] — 2026-09-30

### Added
- **Crash reports that reach the developers, every time.**
  - Every kind of failure is reported. Java crashes send their stack and breadcrumbs. Native crashes send the signal, the library, the abort message and the readable part of the tombstone. ANRs send the thread dump. Each also carries the app version and the last thing the app was doing, which Android keeps with its record of the death.
  - Reports are sent automatically from an outbox, by a system job scheduled before the crashing process dies. The job retries with backoff until the report is filed, across reboots, whether or not the app is opened again.
  - Automatic sending is on unless the user turns it off (Settings → Crash report). The first time a report is sent, the user is told once. Reports carry the error, the breadcrumbs, the device model and the app version, and nothing from configs or traffic.
  - Reports go to a crash collector of their own (`worker-src/crash`): a Worker with no database, on a different Cloudflare account from the Quick Connect pool, that files straight into the private GitHub repository. The pool's `/crash` remains the fallback. One issue per bug; at most one comment an hour, with that report in full; a closed issue reopens when a newer build hits it.
  - `/health` on the collector, and a `github` block in the pool's `/crashes`, show whether GitHub filing works. An expired token used to fail without a trace.
- **Home screen: folders, the iOS way.**
  - Hold an app over another until a plate grows behind it, then let go: the two become a folder in the target's place. It opens at once, named after what is in it (Connections, Configs, Tools, Games, Emergency) until it is renamed.
  - Hold an app over a folder: the folder swells, and letting go adds the app at the end. Keep holding and the folder springs open, so the app can go anywhere inside it.
  - Drag an app out past an open folder's panel: the folder closes behind it, and the app can land anywhere on the board, onto another app or into another folder. A folder whose last app leaves is removed.
  - While rearranging, folders wear iOS's minus badge. Removing a folder asks first, then puts its apps back where the folder was.
  - The name is a text field with a clear button while rearranging. Outside rearranging, holding the name starts renaming.
  - More than nine apps page sideways inside a folder. Holding an icon at the edge of the board or of a folder turns the page. Right-to-left layouts are mirrored throughout.
- **MAE: self-healing.** A route could die under an app and nothing looked. After ~20 minutes idle, Instagram's videos stopped until the user reconnected by hand. MAE now sends one small request through the running tunnel itself: into its own loopback inbound, through the rules and the app's balancer, out of the route the app is using. This runs 8 s after connecting, every 5 minutes with the screen on, and on unlock after 3+ minutes with the screen off. A dead WARP session, or every route dead, heals by reconnecting (at most once in 3 minutes). Anything else fails over live to the next proven route and is re-checked first. The app's row says what happened.
- **MAE: foreign exits from the user's own Cloudflare panels.** For apps that refuse Iranian addresses, WARP, WARP-in-WARP, WireGuard and MASQUE are no use: they all exit in Iran.
  - MAE takes configs automatically from the panels on the user's Cloudflare accounts (BPB, EDG, MLM, Netra, then GZG, NVA, NHN), through the same adapters the config arena uses.
  - Each link goes on a clean IPv6 edge first, with IPv4 as the fallback, gated by the network's measured family verdict.
  - Discovery proves every variant per app and keeps the fastest.
  - Links are sealed and read again every 12 h.
- **MAE: whole-app routing (Android 10+).** A chosen app that is installed is routed by its UID (Xray `process` rules, ahead of the domain rules). All of its traffic takes its route: every API domain, fixed IPs and CDNs, not only the domains the registry knows. Each app also has a QUIC balancer; a route that cannot carry QUIC blocks it, so the app falls back to TCP at once.
- **MAE: a Cloudflare account first.** Every foreign exit MAE uses lives on the user's Cloudflare account, so MAE no longer starts without one. With none connected, the screen asks for it first, with the Cloud tab's own form and store: an account added here is the Cloud tab's too.
- **MAE: check again.** "Check all again" on the list (pull-to-refresh does the same) and a check button on every app. Rows swipe like iOS lists: left for Delete, right for Check again; a long swipe acts at once.
- **MAE: an app page.** The manage list opens one page per app: its route as a checkmark list, a switch for whether MAE carries it, Check again, and Remove alone at the bottom behind an iOS alert. The row of identical chips (pause, auto, every route, remove) is gone.
- **Versioned Workers.** The MAE foreign exit, the US exit for Gemini (`GEMINI_EXIT_VERSION`) and the WARP identity relay (`WarpIdRelay.VERSION`) record the build deployed. A current one is not uploaded again on a tap (Reinstall asks first); an older one shows a red badge and "Update". Shared as `WorkerSetupRow` by Settings and the WARP / WireGuard pages.
- **OpenVPN: a real delay test.** After the reset, the probe sends a TLS ClientHello inside the control channel (for TunnelBear cut and window-clamped exactly as `OpenVpnSplitRelay` does) and passes only when the server's certificate flight (2 KB+) or a TLS alert comes back. A server that answers the reset and is then cut shows "Blocked". Eight at a time, with a progress bar and a count.
- **iOS-style update badge.** A red "1" on the Settings icon (dock, board and folders) and a "Software Update Available" row at the top of Settings replace the glyph in the status strip.
- **MAE has its own home-screen icon.**

### Changed
- **Home screen: dragging was rebuilt.**
  - Nothing moves under the finger until it rests. Crossing icons, or pausing on the way to one, no longer shuffles them.
  - What an icon is held over is decided by the icon's middle, not by the finger.
  - While rearranging, an icon is carried by the point where it was touched, not the point the finger had reached when the drag began.
  - A tap on an icon is no longer lost to the board's own "tap empty space to finish" handler.
- **MAE: picking apps no longer rebuilds the tunnel on every tap.** Changes are held while the app picker is open and applied once when it closes, after the new apps' checks. Only newly picked apps are checked (first); every pick used to queue the whole list again. MAE's own apply-reconnect skips the post-connect canary, which could move apps that were fine while the tunnel warmed up.
- **MAE: updating or reinstalling the foreign exit in place** (same address and key) no longer invalidates every app's decisions.
- **MAE: the SIM / time-zone warning was removed from the app rows** (the user found it wrong on a phone where TikTok opened).
- **MAE: TikTok.** Registry v7 marks it `requiresForeign`, so a foreign exit is always probed and required. Its web page answers from Iran, which used to rule a geo restriction out. Its missing API and CDN domains were added.
- **MAE: `gvt1.com` and `ggpht.com` left the Gemini / Flow bundle.** They sent Play Store downloads and YouTube images through the quota-limited US exit.
- **MAE: the repair ladder repairs.**
  - Rung 1 goes abroad at once for a geo-restricted app on a local route, and rung 5 keeps the foreign requirement. Before, rungs 1–2 only rotated local routes, all of them Iranian addresses.
  - The user's evidence ("country refusal", "didn't open on route X") is kept per app and network for 7 days, so the next routine refresh no longer undoes a repair.
  - A repair that changes the route reconnects, so the app's open connections move too. Live switching kept them on the route the user had just said does not work.
  - Cancelling "what's wrong?" no longer uses a rung or marks a route. A 👎 during a routine check is queued, not dropped.
  - Hosts learned from a registry app's site are applied at last.
- **MAE: WARP runs one WireGuard session per core.** Three same-key outbounds per core, plus copies in the probe core, knocked each other off. The probe core has its own WARP identity.

### Fixed
- **Crash reports lost on the pool's busiest days.**
  - Crash reports shared Quick Connect's 60 calls a day, so a heavy user's report was refused (429). They now have their own allowance.
  - On days the pool's D1 ran out of reads, every report was refused, and a failed schema check put Cloudflare's error page in front of every route. Reports from installs the Worker has already verified are now accepted; anything else gets a 503 that the app retries later.
  - With KV out of writes, every report of a known bug commented on its issue again. Each Worker isolate now keeps the dedup records in memory, in front of KV.
- **Crash reports that arrived without the crash.**
  - The text a user shares (Settings → Crash report, and the fallback when sending fails) took the newest three files on the phone. An exit history was written on every launch, so those three were nearly always exit histories: the report said a crash had happened and left out its stack. Stacks now come first.
  - Exit histories are written only when a run ended badly (a crash, an ANR, a kill for resource use), once per exit and only by the main process. Swipes from Recents, updates and the tunnel process's designed exit are not recorded.
  - Old files are pruned by kind, so crash stacks are no longer deleted to make room for notes. Settings counts only real reports.
  - Sending uploads every crash since the last offer (up to three), not only the newest. An ANR or native-crash trace is capped and made readable.
- **Battery: heavy drain while connected, worst with the adaptive engine (MAE).**
  - The VPN service's wake lock was meant to drop after five idle minutes. A connected phone is never idle, so the lock was renewed forever and the CPU never slept. It is now held only during a connect (30 s), and during a real transfer (16 KB/s or more) while the screen is off.
  - MAE: routine re-checks of networks it has already learned wait for the screen to come on; new networks are still learned at once. Its stats and live-check loops sleep while the screen is off, and the WARP keepalive is 25 s instead of 15 s.
  - Traffic counters are sampled every 10 s with the screen off (2 s on). Daily usage is written once a minute instead of on every sample.
  - The other tunnels (MASQUE, WireGuard, WARP, Psiphon, Tor) skip the per-second session write and broadcast while the screen is off. Standalone WARP re-proves its endpoint every 3 minutes instead of every 45 s while the screen is off.
  - Screens out of sight and a backgrounded app no longer poll. Always-on animations (the Quick Connect orb, the Aether halo, the LAN pulse, the drifting lights) run only while on screen.
- **OpenVPN: stuck on "connecting".** A dead server kept the core dialling for ever, and a stop that took longer than 12 s left the page on "connecting"/"disconnecting" until the app was force-stopped. A stop now waits 5 s and then abandons the session (its tunnel and relay closed from outside); a connect not verified within 60 s ends with a reason.
- **MAE: Google's apps (Maps, Gmail, Photos...) could not be ticked in the app picker**, while each tap still re-queued every app. They are now attached to Google (`extraPackages`, routed by UID) and untick cleanly.
- **MAE: changes that never reached the tunnel.** A rotated WARP endpoint, a redeployed Worker or an edited config waited for the next manual connect. Meanwhile, the DoH path's timestamp forced a reconnect (and a FakeDNS reset) every 6 hours.
- **MAE: routes locked out until restart.** The circuit breaker could hold a provider that was never probed, and it judged a route by whether a filtered app answered, so direct went "unavailable" after three filtered apps. It is now per route and network, and judges transport health.
- **MAE: learning without internet.** A network that is not validated, or a captive portal, failed every route and blocked every app. Checks now wait until the network validates.
- **MAE: ChatGPT, Claude and Gemini on a new network** left over an Iranian address until the first check finished. They now take the foreign exit proven on another network, or wait.
- **MAE: a fail-open app is never blocked** while any route is alive.
- **MAE: config routes kept their proofs on the wrong server.** `cfg1..3` were positions in a list that every ping test re-sorts. Ids are now a hash of the config (state schema 2, migrated).
- **MAE: measurement.**
  - Direct RTT no longer includes two DNS lookups.
  - Two families of one route no longer take both throughput slots.
  - A dual-SIM phone keys mobile networks by the data SIM.
  - One transient failure no longer switches the live API off for good.
  - A lost update no longer leaves an app "checking" forever.
- **MAE and MyVpnService:**
  - Auto-switch no longer replaces a MAE session with one node.
  - The watchdog and the Quick Settings tile compile a fresh MAE config for the current network instead of replaying the last one.
  - A scheduled reconnect no longer restarts a tunnel the user stopped.
- **MAE screen:**
  - The engine loads off the main thread.
  - The manage and diagnostics pages no longer rebuild many times a second during checks.
  - The app picker no longer converts icons on the main thread.
  - Routes are named in words.
  - Onboarding keeps typed sites when adding an app.
  - Removing asks first.

### Security
- **The compiled MAE config is no longer stored for the Quick Settings tile.** It carried the WARP key, the Worker's id and the user's configs unsealed, in backed-up preferences. Only the marker `mae` is kept.

## [1.2.37] — 2026-09-30

### Added
- **MAE («موتور تطبیقی MLM», MLM Adaptive Engine), experimental.** A meta-engine on the existing Xray core: the user picks the services they care about, MAE probes each one per network (staged: a few KB per route, RTT for survivors, one throughput sample for the top two), diagnoses on independent axes (censorship, geo restriction, DNS/TCP/TLS interference, service down; a bare 403 is never a verdict), and routes each service separately on top of the unchanged Serverless v48 config -- one balancer per service, so a repair re-points that service only (`OverrideBalancerTarget` over a Unix-socket API when the one-time spike proves it works on the phone; reconnect otherwise). Foreign exits are candidates until the service's own probe passes through them per address family; the first is a VLESS-over-WebSocket Worker deployed to the user's own Cloudflare account (`assets/mae_egress_worker.js`, new code). "Didn't open" is an incident that re-probes and re-routes at top priority. Measured on the phone (MCI, 2026-09-29): Serverless/fragment reached nothing, so MAE picked a working DoH (`8.8.8.8` direct) and sent unknown traffic direct; ChatGPT and Claude were served on IPv4 and refused on IPv6, so they go direct over IPv4; Gemini, YouTube, Instagram, Facebook and Telegram go through the Worker (exits BG/RO); a Gemini route switch during a YouTube stream kept all 25 YouTube responses on one connection; no measurable overhead versus no VPN. Architecture and status: `MAE_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `MAE_IMPLEMENTATION_STATE.md`.

- **MAE: Gemini and Google Flow through the "US exit".** A Worker leaves from the Cloudflare colo nearest the phone, and Google places that in Iran (Gemini's page carries `"rtQCxc":-210`, Iran's time zone) and refuses every prompt ("Something went wrong (1060)"). MAE now uses the app's US exit (a Durable Object in North America on the user's own Cloudflare account) as a route of its own, reached through the user's Cloudflare config that works on the network; it is set up automatically the first time Gemini or Flow is checked, through a working route where Cloudflare's API is blocked (Irancell). While either needs a foreign exit, all of Google's account domains (`googleapis.com`, `google.com`, `gstatic.com` ...) go with it -- the Gemini app's backend is `robinfrontend-pa.googleapis.com` -- and YouTube keeps its own route. The probe reads Google's own verdict from the page (`FL1an` / `MuJWjd` / `rtQCxc`). Measured on the phone: the Gemini app answered on Irancell; Flow opened on MCI with its traffic through the US exit.
- **MAE: Google Flow** (`flow.google.com`); its Android app (`com.google.android.apps.labs.whisk`) is recognised, and an app added from the installed list before the registry knew it becomes the registry's entry.
### Fixed
- **MAE: smoother screen, fewer tunnel drops.** State is written off the main thread and batched; the rows, the network key and the manage page's route list are worked out off the main thread; connect builds its config in the background. Reconnects needed by background checks are made once, after the checks quiet down, instead of one per learned domain.
- **MAE: a failed proof no longer wipes a good exit.** An exit that merely missed one echo keeps its proof for a day, and an app that must not look Iranian never falls back to a local route.
- **Gemini exit Worker** writes a copy of the first payload, never a view of the request buffer.
- **Google Script («گوگل اسکریپت»): "download error" when installing the certificate on Android 8/9.** The copy into the public Downloads folder needs a storage permission the app does not hold. Below Android 11 the system's own certificate installer is now opened with the certificate loaded (as domain fronting already did).
- **OpenVPN crash on Android 8 ("Socket Closed" on `ovpn-relay-down`).** The relay's helper threads read a socket the other side had just closed, outside any catch. They now catch and end quietly.
- **VPN stayed down after a network drop (every engine on MyVpnService).** The watchdog's own STOP called `stopSelf`, which destroyed the service -- and the reconnect scheduled 3 s later with it. The internal-reconnect STOP no longer stops the service; measured on the phone: Wi-Fi off/on reconnects in ~3 s.

### Security
- **Cloudflare credentials are sealed with an Android Keystore key** (AES-256-GCM, `data/SecureStore.kt`); the plaintext account list is migrated on first load and deleted. Cloud accounts and MAE state are excluded from Android backup and device transfer. Crash reports are passed through `SecretRedactor` (Global API keys, Bearer and 40-char tokens, auth headers, emails, secret-named parameters) before they are written, shared or uploaded.

## [1.2.36] — 2026-09-26

Config Studio engine build 22 (schema 19). The engine-side fixes reach an account once its engine is updated from More › Cloudflare accounts.

### Added (engines, home screen)
- **Geph («گف»).** Geph's own Android build of geph5-client, unmodified: `libgeph.so` taken from the official 5.9.0 APK after verifying Geph's minisign-signed release manifest (their key, as hard-coded in `updates.rs`) and the APK's signed sha256 (`scripts/install-geph-binary.js`); the store updates it through the same chain (`StoreSource.SignedManifest`, `Minisign.kt`, BLAKE2b-512 prehash). The TUN is handed to the child as its stdin (`--vpn-fd 0`, the official app's method); Android 7 goes through tun2proxy to its SOCKS port instead. The tunnel takes `0.0.0.0/0` and `::/0` (MTU 16384, Geph's ipstack segment size) so no family leaks, and the app excludes itself. Control is Geph's newline JSON-RPC over a unix socket, never an unauthenticated TCP port. "Connected" requires `conn_info` = Connected and a real TLS request through an internal `port_forward` to `www.gstatic.com:443`. A process death or a default-network change restarts the engine with the TUN kept (up to 4 times); a send-without-receive stall is tested and restarted. The cache is one file per account, because the network hands out 20 connect tokens a day and a cold cache spends one per start. Account: free account by proof-of-work, sign-in with a code (current/retired/invalid via `get_account_secret_status`), Free/Plus/Basic with expiry and data left, rotating a code (codes starting with 9), free voucher and redeeming a gift code; no purchase. Exit country/city with load and the streaming category, a fastest-server finder that probes sequentially on a copy of the cache, a speed test, direct connection to the exit, ad/adult filters, proxy-only coverage, TCP port forwards, LAN sharing, Geph's news, and sessions with the live log.
- **WARP («وارپ»), the standalone engine.** Separate from the engine behind MASQUE, WireGuard and WARP-in-WARP, as on Windows: its own identity (registered directly, or through the user's Worker where Cloudflare's API is filtered, then `PATCH warp_enabled:true` -- a fresh registration passes nothing until asked), its own data directory and SOCKS port (20870), `libaether.so` with `AETHER_PROTOCOL=wg`. The TUN is opened only after `cdn-cgi/trace` answers `warp=on` through it (150 s budget); a re-check every 45 s drops the remembered endpoint after two failures and restarts the engine with the TUN kept. Settings: scan mode, address family, obfuscation, keepalive -- each reaches the engine.
- **OpenVPN («OpenVPN»), TunnelBear servers.** The user's own TunnelBear account, direct: the session goes over TCP 7011 through `OpenVpnSplitRelay`, a loopback relay that dials the server itself (protected, no second tunnel) and cuts the opening records into misaligned segments. Round button, live card, server and account pages, flags and Persian country names, no dialogs; "connected" only after real data. Cipher, compression and the usage direction fixed; the native core logs with credentials scrubbed. `libmlmopenvpn.so` is 16 KB page-aligned, like every other native library. **One name is a pool:** each country's hostname stands for ~20 servers and DNS returns another on each lookup; a phone-side watch (5 rounds x 47 names, 2026-09-27) found whole subnets silent every round while their siblings answered (Norway 0/5, UK 0/5, Ireland's 146.70.227.x, most of 38.248.89.x in US-East). The delay test now sends the reset to up to six addresses in parallel (the fresh lookup plus the last healthy ones) and stores the answering ones fastest first (`ProbeResult.healthy`); the relay dials those in order with a 4 s connect each and moves the next one to the front whenever a connection brings back less than a certificate flight, so the core's own retry lands on another server. Measured on the phone: connected in ~4 s to a healthy Latvian address, 11.5 Mbit/s down, IPv6 unreachable inside the tunnel (no leak).
- **Home-screen folders, and «ابزارها».** Fixed IP, Sub link, LAN sharing, Usage and Tutorials move into a Tools folder, on fresh installs and (once) in saved layouts. Holding an icon over another for 420 ms makes a folder; inside, rename, reorder and drag out. Layout rules are pure functions with unit tests (`BoardLayoutRules`).
- **One icon set with Windows.** `scripts/icons-from-windows.js` evaluates the Windows app's `public/shell/icons.js` and writes the shared icons as VectorDrawables (`--check` reports drift); Psiphon keeps its Android icon.

### Removed
- **«تونل ورسل».** The Vercel proxy it routed through had stopped answering; the tile, its screen, the interceptor on eleven HTTP clients and the dead relay in VPN Gate are gone, and the old preference is cleared once.

### Added (Cloud)
- **Nova (Nova Proxy) panel** (`engines/nova/NovaPanel.kt`). PolyForm Noncommercial and shipped obfuscated, so never bundled: the install fetches `worker.js` from IRNova/Nova-Proxy pinned to its commit and refuses it unless it matches that commit's `version.json` `worker_sha256`. D1 as `DB`, KV as `KV`, `nodejs_compat`, compat date 2026-07-30. A fresh Nova has no password and a public `/install`, so the app claims it at once (`POST /install/set`, random 24 chars). Configs: `POST /login` → `GET /admin/config.json` (HOST, UUID) → token `md5(md5(HOST+UUID)[7:27])` (the panel's `He`) → `GET /sub?token=…&b64`; Nova's own `/admin/sub-content` makes the Worker fetch itself, which Cloudflare refuses on workers.dev (error 1042). Store: `GithubFile` on main. Cloud row, lamp, arena adapter, `NVA` badge. Measured: installed, claimed, 17 VLESS configs fetched over the IPv6 route.
- **Worker route everywhere a Worker is called:** `CloudManager` (BPB/Edge config fetch), Edge settings, MLM API/deployer, Nahan deployer, sub generator, the MLM pool, the Google-Script deployer, game crowd and dedicated DNS clients now use `WorkerRoute` (healthy-IP DNS, HTTP/1.1); BPB and Edge "get configs" had kept timing out on the silent IPv4.
- **Usage chart on Iran's calendar:** this week Saturday–Friday in `Asia/Tehran`, hourly buckets read as Tehran days, Persian date via ICU `fa_IR@calendar=persian`, average over elapsed days.
- **Settings › Software update › «آخرین تغییرات»** (when up to date): `UpdateChecker.latestReleaseNotes` reads `/releases/latest` (public only) on every open and renders the body (headings, bullets, bold); offline it shows the newest `changelogVersions` entry.
- **Config Arena («میدان کانفیگ»)** (`engines/arena/*`, `ui/arena/*`). One adapter per panel (`ArenaPanel`) over the existing deploy/fetch functions; preparation installs what is missing and updates what the Store marks behind on the account, after one confirmation page, with the Store's own job (no second update path). Rounds on existing primitives, round-robin so network drift hits everyone: qualifier (parse → TCP → TLS+SNI → one real request via `CombineEngine.measureUri`), latency ×3 (median), reach (a Cloudflare-hosted URL through the config), and in Full exact 1 MB throughput (OVH, sequential) and 6 stability samples 3 s apart. Scoring is ratio-to-best per round, weights 35/20/25/20 renormalised over the rounds that ran; categories only with ≥2 measurements (`ArenaScoreTest`). History (last 50) with time and network; `ArenaStore.latest(network)` counts as current only on the same network within 24 h. Measured on the phone: Quick with 7 panels in ~1.5 min (Edge and Nahan installed during preparation), Full in ~3.5 min. Found on the way: a Studio user is born with no configs, so the arena user gets the New-user screen's defaults (`LocationConfigs.create`); the track is forced left-to-right under RTL; `realSpeedTest`'s one-decimal MB/s made every panel 3.2 or 4.0 Mbit/s, so the arena measures exactly. The race scenes (grid reveal, F1-style start lights, a live left-to-right track, podium with a one-shot confetti) are drawn in the system dark palette: neutral tiles per team, colour only where it means something (red lights, green GO, red retirement, gold/silver/bronze, blue progress). Light sound effects are synthesised with `AudioTrack` (`ui/arena/ArenaSounds.kt`, no audio files), with a mute toggle remembered per device. **One clean IP for all** (`engines/arena/ArenaCleanIp.kt`, phase `CLEAN_IP`, always on — the user's rule): candidates are this network's last winner, the scanner's unified archive (newest 30) and 30 random addresses from Cloudflare's ranges; TCP 443 → the quickest 10 get 5 TLS handshakes with a competitor's SNI (any failure is out, rank = median + spread) → the steadiest 3 carry the same exact 1 MB download, one at a time; the fastest wins. Every competitor's configs are rewritten onto it with `CombineEngine.rewrite` (worker name kept as SNI/host) before the three candidates are picked; the IP is remembered per network, merged into the archive, and saved in the session (`ip`). Before this, BPB and Netra raced on clean Cloudflare IPs from 12–16 variants while MLM raced on its bare workers.dev hostname with 2. The start lights use flat square-wave beeps (one E5 per light, a long E6 at lights-out) rather than the countdown tick; the result page opens on the same 2-1-3 podium (forced LTR), with scores on the steps.
- **Netra** (`engines/netra/NetraPanel.kt`): a BPB fork that reads `UUID`/`TR_PASS`/`SUB_PATH` from the environment first — deployed with random ones plus a `kv` KV, `nodejs_compat`; configs from `/<SUB_PATH>/sub/raw?app=xray` (12 on the phone). Its code carries BPB's `EMBEDED_SETTINGS` and `panelVersion:"5.1.1"`, so the Store lists Netra before BPB and BPB's detector excludes it — otherwise BPB's code would have been pushed over a Netra install.
- **Gozargah** (`engines/gozargah/GozargahPanel.kt`): the developer's `gozargah-worker.js` with a D1 bound as `GZ_DB`; first run logs in with the shipped `admin`, then sets a random password, panel path and sub path; a dedicated `mlmvpn-arena` user; configs from `/{subPath}/{token}?app=v2ray`. Store: GithubRelease, version from `VERSION`. Shared Cloudflare calls in `engines/cloud/CfWorkers.kt`; Cloud rows in `ui/cloud/PanelRows.kt` (an early `return@Column` there crashed Compose and was replaced with if/else).
- **Spider panel («پنل اسپایدر»).** SpiderPanel (amirh00sain/SpiderPanel) is a Python panel for a VPS; only its Worker runs on Cloudflare, and that Worker is self-contained behind a Bearer-token admin API (`/panel/config`, `/panel/status`, `/panel/health-check`, `/api/users`), so the app is its panel (`engines/spider/SpiderPanel.kt`, `ui/spider/SpiderScreen.kt`). Deploy: the developer's file from GitHub (the repo has no licence, so it is not bundled; the store's pinned copy once there is one), `__PANEL_TOKEN__` / `__PANEL_DOMAIN__` / `__WORKER_DOMAIN__` filled as JSON strings, a dedicated `SPIDER_KV` namespace, `workers.dev` enabled; the status retries for the few seconds a new name takes to answer. Brought over only what the app lacked: per-user concurrent-IP limits, and the exit race (two proxies at once, geo/colo-scored EWMA latency, sticky per user, cooldown, health checks) fed with the user's own SOCKS5/HTTP proxies. NOT the panel's default ProxyIP-Daily source: measured, those addresses are Cloudflare relays (a TLS request through them reaches Cloudflare) and none of 12 answered the HTTP CONNECT the Worker sends. `/panel/config` replaces the whole user set, so exits are pushed together with the live users read back first. Users: quota, days, IP limit, reset usage, delete, copy link. "Get configs" in the Cloud card (and on the page) files the usable users as an `SPD` group and makes the combine offer, like every other panel (`ui/spider/SpiderConfigs.kt`). The Worker lists users with KV `list()`, which is eventually consistent, so a user just created was missing until the page was reopened; the app now lays its own writes (create, change, delete) over the listed users for 90 s. Measured on the phone: deploy, user, connect; 8.6 Mbit/s from OVH, exit 104.28.159.17 (Cloudflare's egress); with no exit, Cloudflare-hosted sites get no reply (a Worker cannot `connect()` to Cloudflare), which the page states.
- **Store: Spider's Worker from the developer's GitHub** (`StoreSource.GithubFile`, `worker/worker.js` on `main`, pinned to its commit). `WorkerSpec` gained `inject` (carry the deployed copy's token and domains into the new code) and `normalize` (put them back as placeholders before hashing). Normalising must restore every occurrence: the header comment names the placeholders too and both deployers fill it; restoring only the `const` lines made every installed copy read as out of date.

### Changed
- **Cloudflare IPv4 → IPv6 self-healing** (`data/CfFamily.kt`, `data/CfEdgeHeal.kt`, `data/ScanScout.kt`, `engines/cloud/WorkerRoute.kt`). Measured on the phone 2026-09-28 with a VLESS-over-WS probe through the user's own Worker: on Cloudflare IPv4 the TLS handshake and the WebSocket upgrade (101) succeeded and **no data came back** (plain HTTP or TLS inside), while on Cloudflare IPv6 a ServerHello came back in 1.1–1.9 s; 48/54 random v6 addresses in 16 CF prefixes answered. `CfFamily` keeps a per-network verdict (20 min) from REAL Xray tests (both families in parallel) and samples v6 edge addresses from the CF /48s. The scanner scouts every v4 range and the v6 prefixes, settles the family, scans the live family first, and adds a sub-second "alive" check (TLS + the config's own first request) between TCP and the Xray test so silent addresses no longer spend the test budget. `CfEdgeHeal` moves a Cloudflare-fronted config onto a CF v6 edge before connect / delay tests when v4 is dead (SNI/host kept; only a literal IPv4 may prove v4 alive — a name resolves to v6 on its own). `WorkerRoute` (OkHttp `Dns` for `*.workers.dev`) probes each address with a real `HEAD /`, returns healthy ones only (v6 first) and forces HTTP/1.1 — HTTP/2 coalescing across the account's shared certificate had kept requests on a silent IP; it fixed MLM/Spider/Netra/Gozargah config fetches (the MLM "engine hang" on `/users/{id}/configs` was this). GitHub Tunnel (`GtCleanIp`, broker client), the arena's clean-IP step, the tunnel core's `ip_scan` and Aether's `AETHER_IP` take both families (auto: v6 when v4 is measured dead, both when unknown, explicit user choice kept). `CfUsage.failures/hourly` log Cloudflare's per-script invocation statuses (`CfErrors`). Verified on the phone: scan 10/10 healthy in 26 s, IPv6 connect with exit on Cloudflare and 1 MB in 1.9–2.9 s, an IPv4 config healed on connect, all 7 panels' configs fetched, GitHub Tunnel over v6 at 2.2 Mbit/s; MASQUE / WireGuard / WARP-in-WARP / WARP connected on this network at 2.8–6 Mbit/s.
- **Cloud tab: daily-usage module and folding accounts** (`ui/cloud/CloudUsageModule.kt`, `engines/cloud/CfUsage.kt`). Seven UTC days of `workersInvocationsAdaptive` requests per account (bucketed by `datetimeHour`, summed per UTC day, all accounts in parallel, cached 5 min), drawn as Screen-Time-style stacked bars with a dashed daily average, a selectable day, and a per-account bar against the free 100,000 (orange ≥80%, red ≥95%); an account whose token cannot read analytics says so instead of drawing zero. With more than one account, `AccountGroupCard` folds to its header row (`collapsed`/`onToggleCollapsed`), one open at a time. Panel rows (`CloudPanelHeaderRow`) carry the panel's tinted squircle mark and a state pill (installed / update / not installed / spinner) instead of a status glyph, on the card itself rather than a `BgDark` strip. The received groups moved from the foot of each card to one page-level module (`ui/cloud/CloudReceivedModule.kt`) with the account's chart colour, so a folded card hides none of them; rows open the same group page. No existing row, action or dialog was removed.
- **Config Studio engine build 19, tried and rolled back (build 20).** Stale-while-revalidate credentials (a known user connecting from the cache while the row is re-read beside it) plus the first 16 KB of each connection sent unbuffered. Measured on the phone with two Full arena races before and after: MLM's 1 MB download was cut off ("connection closed") in both races and the first response through it went from ~190 ms to ~380 ms, so build 20 is build 18's data plane again with only the number moved (`STUDIO_API_VERSION` = `PanelBuild.MLM` = 20), which lets the Store replace 19 where it was installed. Build 21 tried the unbuffered 16 KB alone: the download no longer broke (so the cut-off was the credential cache), but latency was no better (205/208 ms against 132–181), so build 22 is build 18's data plane again.
- **Gateway: offline first, the network only on request.** `VpnGateRepository.refresh()` without `force` now publishes the last good fetch, else the shipped list, whatever its age, and never fetches on its own (it used to whenever the cache was over a week old or missing -- the test phone had no cache at all, so every visit went online). The shipped list is refreshed by `scripts/update-vpngate-seed.js`, which also writes `default_servers.fetched` so its age shows instead of «نامعلوم»; the "old, update once" notice now appears only for a stale or unstamped copy. The update got faster on the phone, 56 s to 17 s: the direct route is dropped at once when the system resolver's IPv4 answer is private (the block page, 10.10.34.36, came with a matching AAAA `2001:4188:2:600:10:10:34:36`), and DoH goes to `8.8.8.8` by address first (`cloudflare-dns.com` and `dns.google` resolve to the block page; 1.1.1.1 and 1.0.0.1 got no reply).
- **GitHub Tunnel: setup in three steps, in order.** Cloudflare first (open the Cloud tab when no account is connected, otherwise install the relay Worker on it, with the account choice beside the step when there are several), then GitHub, then connect. The dial performs the current step; exit country, rules and accounts stay hidden until setup is done, and the Worker section appears once it is installed.

### Fixed
- **Every tunnel screen read «آماده» the moment a connect failed.** `TunnelController` cleared `active` on FAILED as well as DISCONNECTED, so `TransportScreen` no longer owned the tunnel, showed IDLE, and never rendered the failure line or `IdentityBlockedCard`. A failure now keeps its owner (every other reader of `active` also requires RUNNING or busy).
- **«وارپ» identity: the Worker before the front.** Measured on the phone (Iran, Wi-Fi): with `api.cloudflareclient.com` as the SNI no Cloudflare address answers the ClientHello, whole or cut into 1-29 byte segments -- the filter reassembles -- while `www.cloudflare.com` on the same addresses completes in under a second, and Cloudflare answers a mismatched SNI/Host with 403. `registerStandalone` now tries direct, then the user's Worker, then the camouflaged front, instead of spending four read timeouts (~50 s) first. Through a healthy Worker (`/_health` 200) Cloudflare answered the registration with 429 / error 1015 for ~3 minutes, then accepted it: on a 429 the Worker is asked once more after the front, and the error names the rate limit instead of the front's timeout. End to end on the phone: identity via the Worker, `warp=on` 38 s after the tap, 40 Mbit/s down, IPv4 and IPv6 from Cloudflare's WARP ranges (`loc=IR` is WARP's own geolocation), DNS `1.1.1.1`. Geph measured on the same phone: connected in under 2 s (exit LT), IPv4 and IPv6 both leave from Geph's exit, DNS is the tunnel's 1.1.1.1; download is 1.6 Mbit/s in one stream and the same 1.6 in total over three, i.e. the Free plan's account-wide cap, not the route.
- **Configs kept dropping.** XHTTP packet-up sessions were deleted 60 s after creation whatever their activity, later POSTs lost their data, POSTs were not re-ordered, and the bridge ran inside POST 0's `waitUntil`, which Cloudflare stops about 30 s after the response. Sessions now expire on inactivity, POSTs are re-ordered by `seq`, and the bridge runs inside the GET's streaming response. That packet-up engine is kept, but **XHTTP is paused**: measured against a live workers.dev installation, Cloudflare answers the gRPC-typed request Xray sends for stream-one with 403 and holds any other request body until it is complete, and packet-up's upload POSTs sent while its download is open land in other instances of the Worker and are lost. No screen creates XHTTP any more and the subscription leaves existing XHTTP configs out; making it work needs a Durable Object for each connection's requests to meet in. WebSocket and Trojan no longer wait on D1 or the Durable Object before the 101 (isolate caches for settings, served route keys and credentials), the connect timeout is 3 s instead of 1 s, the socket closes only after the downstream is flushed, IPv6 destinations are accepted, DNS goes over DoH in parallel, and rendered WebSocket links carry early data (`?ed=2560`).
- **Usage the operator never saw (10 MB used, 60 KB shown).** Traffic writes never moved `users.updated_at`, the app syncs on `?since=updated_at`, and the tail of a session stayed in isolate memory. One usage meter (`07a-meter.js`) now serves WebSocket, Trojan and XHTTP: it flushes at a threshold, every 90 s while bytes are pending and on the last close, bumps `updated_at`, and writes `usage_daily` in the same batch, so per-person stats match the person's volume. The app re-syncs with a two-minute overlap and the user page writes its fresh copy back into the index.
- **A 100 MB user used 108 MB.** The commit threshold was a tenth of the cap in each isolate; it is now an eighth of the remaining room (64 KiB to 32 MiB), admission reads the byte columns (which also enforces a plan's daily cap, never read before), and running out closes every connection of that person in the isolate.
- **Multi-location configs left from the wrong country, or not at all.** Exits were chosen per connection and by the country they were filed under. Each country config now carries a pinned exit (`configs.exit_pin`) chosen from verified candidates of the measured country, fails over to another in the same country and writes the new pin back. Public pool servers are used only after a probe shows they really leave from that country (`pool_verified`). The ports chosen for a person now apply to every endpoint in their subscription.
- **Device limits were recorded, not enforced.** The Durable Object counts the devices present in the last 150 s (a device silent for 90 s gives its slot up, so a phone moving from Wi-Fi to mobile data gets back in), is asked once per device per isolate instead of per connection, and XHTTP goes through it as well. The connection count is folded into the device limit; users with a device limit become strict in migration 19. The deploy reads the script's real migration tag instead of one remembered on the phone, so a second phone no longer strips the Durable Object.
- **One phone counted as two devices.** The device fingerprint included `Sec-WebSocket-Version`, which a WebSocket upgrade carries and an XHTTP request does not, so a person using both protocols filled a one-device limit alone. It is the client's name and network now.
- **Refusals the activity feed could not name** -- a blocked device, torrent, mail, a private address, a connection flood -- read as a bare «رد شد»; each has its own line now.
- **A failed request on the user page read as "not enforced".** The devices section now has three states: could not read, this account has no device counter, or the real figures.
- **"0 = remove the cap" in bulk edits left the cap in place.** Cleared fields are sent as explicit nulls.
- **The Cloud tab's MLM panel could break Config Studio.** It is the same Worker and database, but its install/update ran an older upload that replaced the Worker's settings with three of its own: Config Studio's route (and with it every `/s/` link), the Durable Object and the key were dropped, and `DEBUG=1` wrote a database row per request.
